#2841. Democrat Mary Peltola wins Alaska Senate election

Democratic candidate Mary Peltola won the Alaska Senate election, capping a midterm cycle that already was one of the best for Democrats in more than a half-century, the Associated Press reported Tuesday evening.

Peltola defeated incumbent Republican Sen. Dan Sullivan in a race that was surrounded by controversy over an independent candidate who shared his name.

Peltola had faced accusations that her campaign recruited ultra-longshot candidate Dan J. Sullivan to the race to confuse voters. The Justice Department filed a lawsuit seeking to delay certification of the election if the incumbent Sullivan lost in order to conduct an investigation into whether voter confusion tainted the outcome.

The Justice Department is appealing the dismissal of that civil suit. The department is also conducting a criminal investigation into the second Sullivan's decision to enter the race.

Peltola's victory gives Democrats a solid majority of 56 seats in the Senate beginning in January.

Democrats this cycle flipped nine Senate seats that are currently held by Republicans.

That outcome, which was not considered remotely likely at the beginning of the election, came as part of a massive Democratic wave this year, which saw the party also flip majority control of the House of Representatives by a decisive margin.

Alaska was the last Senate race to be called in the midterm elections. Peltola, a former House member, joins Senator-elect Peggy Flanagan as the first two Native American women elected to the Senate.

The race was called two weeks after Election Day, and six days after Peltola's fellow Democrat Scott Colom pulled off perhaps the most stunning upset of the 2026 cycle by defeating incumbent Republican Sen. Cindy Hyde-Smith in the Mississippi Senate race.

As of 10:39 p.m. ET, Peltola had 50.1% of the vote, and Sullivan had 45.8%. Another Republican, Gerald Heikes, had 2.1% of the vote, while Daniel J. Sullivan had 1.6% of the vote.

If no candidate exceeded 50% of the vote, the election would have been decided through a ranked-choice method, in which the two bottom finishers would have been eliminated, and the second choices on the ballots of voters who selected them would be allocated to the remaining two candidates.

"From Juneau to Utqiagvik, the message from Alaskans is clear: the status quo in DC is not working for Alaska," Peltola said in a statement.

"Over the course of this campaign I've spoken with elders in tears about empty fish racks; families struggling to find housing they can afford; and parents in the Bush faced with the impossible choice of heating their homes or putting food on the table," Peltola said. "Today we take a monumental step forward in addressing those issues and putting Alaska first."As a lifelong Alaskan who has dedicated my life to serving our state, there is no greater honor than having Alaskans' support in our fight to take on corporate greed, lower costs, and put Alaska first," she said. "Thank you Alaska. Let's get to work."

#2839. Raiders provide crucial update on tight end Brock Bowers

Brock Bowers remains day-to-day with an ankle injury and did not practice Wednesday as the Raiders began preparing for Sunday's game against the host Broncos.

Raiders coach Klint Kubiak said Wednesday that Bowers is progressing in his recovery but remains "day-to-day."

Brock Bowers of the Las Vegas Raiders leaves the field after an injury against the Seattle Seahawks in Las Vegas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

His availability remains a question heading into Fernando Mendoza's second NFL start. Having Bowers on the field would give the rookie quarterback a trusted target against another challenging defense.

The Raiders got a painful reminder of his importance during Sunday's loss to the Seahawks.

Bowers injured his ankle on the opening series, briefly returned and then left for the remainder of the game. His departure stripped Mendoza of one of the NFL's best tight ends and allowed Seattle to turn up the defensive pressure without having to account for the Raiders' most dangerous receiving threat.

For a quarterback making his first career start, it made an already difficult assignment considerably tougher.

The Seahawks sacked Mendoza six times, and the Raiders struggled to establish a running game that could ease the burden on their passing attack. Losing Bowers further reduced their options as Seattle took control.

The hope is that he can return against Denver. His absence Wednesday leaves the Raiders waiting to see how his ankle responds as the week progresses.

If Bowers cannot play, Michael Mayer would take on greater importance at tight end, while Tre Tucker and the rest of the Raiders' receivers would face added responsibility to create opportunities for Mendoza.

Brock Bowers #89 and Fernando Mendoza #15 of the Las Vegas Raiders wait to be introduced before a game in Las Vegas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

That means winning individual matchups, giving the quarterback dependable targets and making enough plays to keep Denver from dictating the terms of every possession.

Bowers' value extends beyond the passes he catches. The attention he commands can create room for teammates, and his ability to work different areas of the field gives the Raiders flexibility in how they attack a defense.

Replacing that would require contributions throughout the offense.

Las Vegas Raiders head coach Klint Kubiak walks onto the field for warmups before a game at Allegiant Stadium in Las Vegas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

Mayer can provide a target in the middle of the field. Tucker offers speed to threaten downfield. The Raiders will need both elements, along with better protection and a more productive running game, to help Mendoza settle into his first road start.

For now, they will monitor Bowers' ankle while preparing for the possibility of facing the Broncos without their most important receiving weapon.

#2837. Nets vs. Bulls Preview: Nic Claxton Returns to Brooklyn

Well, Nets fans, Wednesday night at Barclays Center might feel a little strange.

Nic Claxton is coming back to Brooklyn. Only this time, he'll be wearing a Chicago Bulls uniform.

After spending seven seasons with the Nets, Claxton returns to the building he once called home. While the reunion provides a little extra intrigue, both teams have bigger problems to worry about. Brooklyn and Chicago enter Wednesday with identical 3-10 records, each searching for some much-needed momentum.

Something's gotta give, right? Perhaps the Nets will channel some old fortune from the ABA days on ABA Night. It's the first of six nights dedicated to different eras in Nets history.

Who: Chicago Bulls (3-10) at Brooklyn Nets (3-10)

When: Wednesday, November 18, 2026, 7:30 p.m. ET

Neither team is exactly setting the Eastern Conference on fire.

The Nets are coming off Monday's 135-123 loss to Philadelphia, their fourth defeat in five games. Julius Randle delivered 33 points, while rookie Nolan Traore chipped in 23.

Unfortunately, another productive offensive night wasn't enough.

Brooklyn found itself trailing by as many as 24 points before showing some life in the second half. And that's becoming a familiar storyline for Jordi Fernandez's group. The Nets have shown flashes of competitive basketball. The problem? Putting together 48 minutes of it.

There are bigger problems of course. Rotations aren't consistent and the team isn't playing hard. Scouts believe the roster wasn't constructed properly. Whatever it is, it ain't working.

Fortunately, Chicago isn't arriving in much better shape.

The Bulls recently snapped an eight-game losing streak with consecutive victories over Atlanta and Minnesota before falling 126-115 to Cleveland on Monday.

Meanwhile, Norman Powell scored 27 points against the Cavaliers, continuing an impressive offensive stretch. He's averaging 28 points over his last three games while shooting 57.1% from the field.

Defensively, though, Chicago has been vulnerable. The Bulls allowed Cleveland to shoot 52.6% from the field Monday, including 69 points in the second half, which presents an opportunity for the Nets.

Brooklyn needs to establish an offensive rhythm early, limit the defensive breakdowns that have plagued its recent outings, and avoid spending another night digging out of a massive hole.

And with both teams sitting at 3-10, Wednesday represents a legitimate chance to get back in the win column.

Joshua Jefferson — OUT (G League Assignment)

Orlando Robinson — OUT (G League Assignment)

Caleb Wilson — OUT (Right metacarpal fracture)

Patrick Williams — PROBABLE (Right finger dislocation)

For seven seasons, Claxton was a familiar presence in Brooklyn's frontcourt, developing from a second-round pick into a defensive anchor and one of the franchise's most recognizable faces.

Traded to Chicago during the offseason as part of the deal that brought Julius Randle to Brooklyn, Claxton missed the Bulls' first eight games with a hamstring injury before making his season debut on November 8. He's played four games since, averaging 7.8 points, 4.5 rebounds and 2.0 assists in 17.9 minutes per night. He's been efficient from the field, converting 61.1% of his attempts — nothing new for Nets fans to see.

Claxton gave Chicago a glimpse of what he can provide offensively with 17 points against San Antonio last week. We'll see if he has the same chip on his shoulder against the team who drafted him.

It's ABA Night at Barclays Center! The Nets will bring all living players and coaches from the franchise's only two title teams (1974, 1976). It aligns with the old jerseys they've been repping along with an alternative court which has the names of every player to ever suit up in a Nets uniform.

#2836. Jensen Huang has agreed to appear before Senate banking committee, Chairman Scott says

Former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang has agreed to appear before the Senate Banking Committee, panel Chair Tim Scott, R-S.C., said on CNBC's "Squawk Box" on Wednesday.

Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick has also agreed to testify, Scott said. The agreements to appear voluntarily come as pressure builds on Capitol Hill to investigate Huang's affair with a suspected Chinese spy, which led to his ouster at the chipmaker he founded.

"They both have agreed," Scott said. "We will hopefully have this done before Christmas."

Huang reached an agreement on Tuesday with the chairmen of three House committees for a closed-door transcribed interview on Dec. 1 jointly before the three panels. Scott had asked Huang to appear publicly in the Senate.

A spokesperson for the banking committee did not immediately respond to questions about when Huang and Lutnick would testify. In initial letters to the pair, Scott requested Huang appear on Dec. 3 and Lutnick appear on Dec. 10.

"Nvidia seemed to have fairly lax controls, and I want to know how much of the relationship with Alice [Huang] impacted Nvidia's unwillingness or inability, or frankly, just blindness to Megaspeed's providing access to China of the most advanced chipset," Scott said of Jensen Huang's testimony.

The details of Huang's appearance before the three House panels on Dec. 1 are still being determined, House Foreign Affairs Committee Chairman Rep. Brian Mast, R-Fla., told reporters at the Capitol on Wednesday. He said the people who will likely conduct the closed-door interview will be the top Republican and top Democrat on his panel and the Select Committee on China and Select Committee on Intelligence along with their attorneys. The location is still being worked out, Mast said.

A date for an appearance before the committees by Jeffrey Kessler, the Commerce official who oversees export controls, is not yet set, Mast said. Nvidia general counsel Tim Teter is scheduled to appear before the panels Dec. 2.

Scott, in his CNBC appearance, also homed in on Lutnick, calling for answers as to why the commerce secretary did not act after being warned about Megaspeed — the Singaporean company where the alleged Chinese spy, Alice Huang, was an executive. Nvidia sold its chips to Megaspeed, which U.S. investigators suspect of diverting them to China.

"They were warned as early as March of this year, several months ago, that Megaspeed should be on the 'entity list,'" Scott said, referring to a Commerce Department list of companies the U.S. views as national security risks. The Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security "didn't do it, Howard didn't do it, we want to know why."

Lutnick and Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent have been embroiled in an intra-administration battle as the fallout from the Huang affair grows. Bessent on CNBC on Friday appeared to blame Lutnick for a lack of action on Megaspeed.

"You will have to ask them about their internal processes," Bessent said, referring to Commerce and noting his department has long supported imposing restrictions on Megaspeed.

A spokesperson for Huang declined to comment on Scott's announcement that he has agreed to appear.

Huang's upcoming appearances are likely just the start of the embattled former CEO's problems on Capitol Hill. Democrats will take control of Congress in January and have demanded sweeping investigations into the affair and Huang's cozy relationship with President Donald Trump.

The House Energy and Commerce Committee asked interim Nvidia CEO Colette Kress for a briefing no later than Dec. 2 on the company's chip exports and information about the relationship between Jensen Huang and Alice Huang. The panel also asked Lutnick and a top deputy to appear to discuss chip export controls. The Wall Street Journal first reported the letters to Kress and Lutnick, and a committee spokesperson confirmed the report to CNBC.

— Megan Cassella and Ashlee Trujillo contributed to this report.

#2828. Fantasy Football Rankings: Hayden Winks' RB Blueprint for Week 11

The Blueprint is my weekly column and basically everything that I use to build out the most accurate rankings I can. There will be this RB column, a WR column and a combination column on the onesie positions. You will find film notes and endless charts. Maybe enough charts to stress test the Yahoo website. I kid.

It's impossible to get every ranking thoroughly early in the week, so be sure to keep checking in on these columns through Sunday morning. As always, my full rankings at every position are available.

1. Jahmyr Gibbs (vs. TB) - 127.5 proj. total yards

Gibbs is the 1.01 in fantasy points over replacement per game (+15.9) and only trails Jaxon Smith-Njigba in winning percentage on Yahoo. Seems good.

Tampa Bay has been stout against the position, ranking 32nd in half PPR points allowed to opposing backfields and holding recent primary rushers like Bijan Robinson (RB28), D'Andre Swift (RB50), Chuba Hubbard (RB34) and Jaylen Warren (RB23) in check. Detroit heads into the home matchup with an implied total of 25.25 points (ninth) as 7-point home favorites. It's a difficult spot, but it's getting nearly impossible to not have him as the RB1 overall every week.

The Chiefs seem to be planning for Kenneth Walker III (groin) to rest another week after Johnson more than held his own in last week's blowout win. The rookie, who reminds me of a Bucky Irving type on film, handled 22 carries and six receptions, leading to 32.1 half PPR points on 20.6 expected points. Those are obviously elite RB1 numbers. Kansas City simply doesn't have another RB it trusts for the moment, and the offense is firing on all cylinders against mediocre opponents.

The Cardinals are just that. They've allowed the fifth-most rush YPG (130) and were just schooled by Blake Corum (119 total yards) last week. A huge 28.0-point team total as 15.5-point favorites (!!!!!!) awaits.

3. James Cook III (vs. MIA) - 110.5 proj. total yards

Cook ranks as the RB6 in half PPR points per game on RB8 usage across his last four contests, averaging 23 carries (second) and 109.8 rushing yards per game. He has scored a rushing touchdown in each of his last four games (what's new?), including a season-high 157 rushing yards on 18 carries in Week 10.

The Bills are expected to beat the Dolphins by 16.5 points with a bonkers 31-point team total. Miami just lost DTs Jordan Phillips and Zeek Biggers to injured reserve. I don't have to tell you this is a great spot.

4. Christian McCaffrey (vs. MIN) - 87.5 proj. total yards

When McCaffrey is in the United States of America this year, he averages 22.7 half PPR points (RB2) on 16.3 expected half PPR points (RB6). Turns out, Kaelon Black is a fine role player but nothing more than that to CMC. Remember to go outside in early September while the weather is nice.

Anyways, this will be a great test for the 49ers as they face the Vikings in Mexico City. They've been run on at times and won't have LB Blake Cashman, but that's about as negative as I can get on what otherwise is an elite defense. They've been fifth best against fantasy RBs this year (16.7 half PPR per game), though fantasy RB1s have held the line; James Cook III (RB7), Jahmyr Gibbs (RB5) and Jonathan Taylor (RB8). We can ding CMC's projection with the Niners projected for 24.25 points versus their current 31.75 points per game, but it can only go so far. He's a mid-range RB1.

5. Chuba Hubbard (vs. BAL) - 109.5 proj. total yards

The Ravens defense has been one of the more underwhelming units. Their secondary has been better than their run defense, to the point that even Omarion Hampton ran well up the gut against them last Monday night. It's a shorter week for Baltimore, who is now trending out of the playoff picture. We'll see how much fight it has left. The Panthers have a 23.0-point team total (12th) as near-field-goal favorites.

6. Javonte Williams (vs. TEN) - 94.5 proj. total yards

Let's keep this simple: Williams is an upside RB1 whenever the Cowboys have a 31.5-point team total, as they do here.

The Titans have allowed nearly 1,500 yards over their last three games, the most in a 3-game stretch in their franchise history. Trading away mega-star DT Jeffery Simmons and Pro Bowl-caliber S Amani Hooker won't help. The starting RB against Tennessee has cleared 100 total yards in 4-straight contests.

7. Bucky Irving (at DET) - 85.5 proj. total yards

This assumes Jalon Daniels is back at QB with Baker Mayfield (foot) picking up a new injury during the bye week. The Bucs are likely going to be extremely run-heavy, as they've been for the past two months with the OL smoking defenses up front. Irving has been the RB7 on RB11 usage this season, with 21.0+ half PPR points in 4 of 5 recent games. He's averaged 22.2 carries during that stretch, with games against pretty easy opponents. That's too big to fail, especially given how slippery he is on tape.

The Lions are a pass funnel typically, but the losses of LB Jack Campbell, EDGE Aidan Hutchinson and CB D.J. Reed (questionable) make them fairly bad in both phases. They still have some DTs to worry about, but the Bucs should be able to run it here, despite the spreadsheets suggesting otherwise. Tampa Bay will attempt to bleed out the clock as 7-point road dogs with a team total of 18.25 points (21st). Irving should absorb a lot of that team total.

8. Derrick Henry (at CAR) - 78.5 proj. total yards

The Ravens are going to pass more with Jacoby Brissett at QB and with the OL struggling, especially when first-round rookie LG Vega Ioane isn't on the field. Henry can still score goal-line touchdowns as he did on Monday night, but a real reset is warranted in terms of efficiency and rushing volume if the Ravens aren't expected to be crushing teams as often as they would've with Lamar Jackson, who is hoping to return around Week 16 if things go to plan. In the meantime, it's possible Henry catches an extra target or two based on the scheme changing, but that won't even out the loss of projected touchdowns. He'll settle in as a boom-bust RB2 the rest of the way.

The Panthers have cleaned things up with better health and a new DC recently, but Carolina has bled fantasy points to RBs (24.8, third) across the season. It was Jonah Coleman (RB6) two weeks ago and Josh Jacobs (RB11) and Bucky Irving (RB2) before that. A 21.0-point team total isn't the worst thing this week, with six teams on bye and five others projected for fewer than 17.0.

9. Jonah Coleman (vs. LV) - 99.5 proj. total yards

With R.J. Harvey (knee) on IR, the backfield has been down to just two. Coleman leapfrogged J.K. Dobbins over the last pair of games, finishing as the RB7 and RB9 in expected half PPR points. Dobbins won't play on passing downs, so Coleman is grabbing some receptions alongside his rushing role. He has been just fine on tape in a good situation.

Speaking of, the Raiders are generous when it comes to fantasy points for RBs. They've allowed, oh, just 176 rushing yards per game this month. Both Seahawks RBs played well behind three backup linemen, and CMC (RB4), Braelon Allen (RB7) and Kyren Williams (RB12) were fantasy RBs this month. Denver is listed as 10-point home favorites with an implied team total of 25.25 points (eighth).

10. Tony Pollard (at DAL) - 85.5 proj. total yards

Pollard has been the RB8, RB3 and RB1 most recently, and it's simply been because he's breaking a ton of tackles. It's hard to believe it'll be sustainable, as star LG Peter Skoronski is on injured reserve, but Pollard is also the RB10 in expected points this month, with Cam Ward taking his game up a tier. It's still a painful offense on the whole and the floor can fall out at any point. It's best to stay tethered to his RB15 on RB16 usage standing across the entire year sample. Hopefully Carnell Tate (shoulder) can return to make the offense more potent.

The Cowboys are pathetic on defense. They've removed their sitting DC from play-calling duties, while their starters have missed a ton of tackles in crucial spots. They also don't play sound against the run, which is why they've allowed the most fantasy points to RBs (27.0) and most rushing yards per game (154) this season. Last week's 44.3 eruption from Christian McCaffrey against them was the best fantasy outing of the year by any player at any position. Beyond that, opponents facing Dallas have passed at a 47% rate in neutral situations, which is the third-lowest neutral pass rate allowed in the league. Tennessee enters as 8.5-point road underdogs with an implied team total of 23 points (13th).

11. Chase Brown (at WAS) - 74.5 proj. total yards

The Bengals have largely abandoned the run, which arguably isn't even a bad thing for Brown. Cincy is up to 30.9 points per game because it's first in passing success rate. Brown has a better chance of lucking into touchdowns if the team passes its way down to the goal line. We saw that happen last week when each of his scores came through the air, both out of structure.

Aside from the scores, Brown tallied eight receptions as a check-down option with space to operate, as defenses are going to have to play softer and softer coverage against these alien WRs. Brown can be forgettable as a talent on a severely pass-heavy offense, and still finish as a low-end RB1 in fantasy due to the scoring bonanza. He's the RB1 in receptions per game (5.2).

The next victim of the Bengals' offense will be the Commanders, who don't have two of their top DTs and were struggling through the air before anyway. The Bengals are up to a 30.5-point team total.

12. D'Andre Swift (vs. NO) - 83.5 proj. total yards

Kyle Monangai (toe) is back. Here are Swift's splits with him since Week 5 of last year, when the offense became the version we have today.

For context, 13.9 half PPR points is the RB16 right now, while 13.0 expected points is the RB17. Those are probably closer to his per-game floor the rest of the way, but it is a real reset for Swift's baseline projection. He can still have RB1 moments with Monangai. They will just happen less often.

The Saints are a great spot to sneak in a fantasy RB1 moment! They've allowed the second-most fantasy points to RBs on the sixth-most rushing yards per game (130). Chuba Hubbard ran right through tackles last week, something that's happened way too often this year. Chicago has a monster 30.0-point team total, so both backs are obvious starts.

13. Jaylen Warren (at PHI) - 95.5 proj. total yards

Warren has settled in as an upside RB2 with Rico Dowdle back in the mix. He's averaged 14.0 carries and 2.8 receptions this month, leading to RB17 on RB19 usage. The Steelers haven't played great ball on offense over that stretch and just watched LG Mason McCormick dislocate his ankle last week. The OL being great this season has certainly helped Warren, who has been a top-5 RB to my eyes this year beyond that.

The matchup is reassuring. This is an eruption spot, looking at the EPA numbers on the ground. The Eagles have struggled all year, then traded away run-stuffing DT Jordan Davis at the deadline. There are fewer reasons to be afraid of what used to be an impossible opponent, and the lines reflect that. Pittsburgh enters as 4.5-point road favorites with an implied team total of 21.5 points (15th).

14. Bhayshul Tuten (at NYG) - 92.5 proj. total yards

Tuten set a career high with 28.1 expected half PPR points last week, with 26 carries and three receptions. Many of those were failed goal-line attempts, but Tuten did score late en route to an RB10 on RB1 usage outing. He's now the RB10 on RB17 usage this season as a low-end RB1 with weekly upside. Chris Rodriguez Jr. will vulture the occasional touchdown, but otherwise, it's all Tuten now. The only marginal issue is that the already suspect Jaguars OL is now missing C Robert Hainsey (knee) for the next couple of weeks.

The Giants rank 25th in half PPR points allowed to opposing RBs at 17.1 points per game. That includes ranking 23rd in points allowed on RB rushing plays (11.3 per game) and 20th on RB receiving plays (5.8 per game). Opposing lead rushers have routinely been held in check against them recently, with no RB clearly the top-24 over their past three games. DROY favorite Arvell Reese has a lot to do with that. Jacksonville heads on the road as 6-point favorites with an implied team total of 26.25 points (seventh). It's more of a mediocre matchup for Tuten.

15. David Montgomery (vs. IND) - 67.5 proj. total yards

Montgomery got in a full practice last Friday before being ruled out, but has practiced in full leading up to Thursday Night Football this week. Woody Marks (concussion) won't play, leaving Monty with Emari Demercado, who missed some lanes in the second half of last game. It won't be a surprise if the desperate Texans use Montgomery as a bell cow despite him being out since Week 5. They don't have a real choice. Of course, the Texans' bell cow RB hasn't been very valuable so far. Since Week 6, the Texans RBs are averaging 16.8 expected half PPR points per game with very mid efficiency. Montgomery finishing with 15-20 expected points shouldn't come as a surprise this week, considering the opponent.

The Colts haven't been competitive all year, especially without their best linemen on both sides of the ball. This month, Indy has allowed the sixth-most rushing yards per game (144). This season, they've allowed the sixth-most points to fantasy RBs (23.0). There really are no excuses for Houston this time around. They own a 28.5-point team total at home as 12.5-point favorites.

16. Omarion Hampton (vs. NYJ) - 71.5 proj. total yards

Hampton set season highs in fantasy points (17.0 half PPR) and usage (15.9 expected) last week, in their first win of the year. Hampton handled 22 carries in a run-heavy game plan that continued because the scoreboard finally allowed for it. The Chargers are first in neutral run rate (61%), so don't be surprised if Hampton's fantasy performances tick up if they can stay more competitive after a brutal schedule. You can see the extra carries in the second half of last week here:

On tape, Hampton was used slightly differently last week. He handled nine pitches, which is the second-most by a player in a single game all year. Instead of being pointed to the perimeter, many of them got him going straight downhill, where his attributes are maximized. They also ran more man concepts instead of the wide zone stuff OC Mike McDaniel typically calls for. It's a meeting in the middle between player and coach, one that was long overdue.

This week, the Chargers are favored. I repeat, the Chargers are favored against the Jets. They don't have their best run-stopping DT right now either, which has helped opposing teams rack up 154 rushing yards per game this month (fourth). James Cook III (RB7) and Kenneth Walker III (RB1) just went nuclear against them, but even Ollie Gordon II had 103 total yards and a score in Week 7, too. This is easily the best it's looked for Hampton since the games started this year.

17. Saquon Barkley (vs. PIT) - 75.5 proj. total yards

Barkley is impossible to get a grasp on due to his injuries, the moving parts on the OL and now Joe Milton at QB. In a part-time role on a lousy offense in three games before the bye, Barkley was the RB25, RB21 and RB20 on RB26 usage. He looked good when he was on the field, and the OL did seem to be in a bit of a better spot on tape. I can see him out-kicking those marks after the bye, which hopefully has allowed him to get healthier. The Eagles' offense is in a spot where they can fully clunker at any time, so the floor doesn't exist.

The matchup will be a challenge, of course. Milton versus the Steelers' pressure will put the team in bad spots. Pittsburgh is only allowing 83 rushing yards per game (30th), as teams have essentially stopped trying to run on them in neutral situations (61% pass, first). Philly can't afford to do that, so it'll be running into brick walls for Barkley, with the hopes he springs a few of them for explosives. A 17.0-point team total works against those odds. Barkley is a volume-based RB2 in the most optimistic of projections.

18. Cam Skattebo (vs. JAX) - 70.5 proj. total yards

Skattebo is back to bell-cow work with Tyrone Tracy Jr. on IR and Najee Harris released. Devin Singletary failed in that role when Skattebo missed in Week 9, then Skattebo delivered an RB5 on RB6 usage outing in Week 10 against the Commanders. They've had extended rest to get Skattebo prepared for another heavy workload in a balanced offense. It won't be surprising to see Skattebo emerge as a fantasy RB2 for the rest of the way, especially if his three grabs from Week 10 hold up.

The Jaguars defense has been stingy to opposing backfields, ranking 29th in half PPR points allowed to RBs. They did concede an RB8 weekly finish to Tony Pollard in Week 10 without DT Jordan Davis and safety Jessie Bates active, though Jonathan Taylor managed just 4.7 points (RB37) against them in Week 8 when they did have a star run-stuffing DT. Opposing offenses have leaned heavily into the pass against Jacksonville with a 59% neutral pass rate (second highest). I doubt the Giants will do that too much given their coaches and personnel, but it's a difficult matchup regardless. New York enters as 6-point home underdogs with an implied team total of 20.25 points (18th).

19. Breece Hall (at LAC) - 70.5 proj. total yards

Hall has been the RB16 and RB21 in usage since returning from a quad injury. He's easily paced Braelon Allen in touches and played more receiving snaps last week after being eased in the game prior. Hall will stick in the 14-18 touch range most outings, while splitting the goal line role with the more downhill Allen. That'll put a cap on Hall's projections each week, but as long as Geno Smith is healthy, the OL is in good enough shape for them to remain balanced on offense. Hall is a fine RB2, while Allen is a shakier FLEX, even during the byes.

The Chargers represent an above-average matchup overall (duh), ranking 11th in half PPR points allowed to opposing RBs at 21.1 per game. Los Angeles is sixth in points allowed on the ground, though they rank 27th against backs via the air. They're still without Derwin James. New York travels out west as 3.5-point road underdogs with an implied total of 19.0 points (19th).

20. Kyle Monangai (vs. NO) - 55.5 proj. total yards

In four games this year, he's the RB16 (14.2) on RB21 usage (12.6 expected).

Fresh off the bye, the Bears should be at the peak of their powers. A date with a Saints team that can't tackle awaits. Chicago projects for 30.0 points. Monangai is an upside FLEX, possibly a straight-up RB2 given the constraints of the week.

21. Ollie Gordon II (at BUF) - 74.5 proj. total yards

The Bills are waking up on defense with a new DC. #2826. Nvidia sinks after earnings, dragging down neoclouds CoreWeave, Iren

Nvidia shares declined another 2.89% Wednesday after the company issued its first earnings report since the departure of former CEO Jensen Huang. The stock is down more than 13% over the past week.

Trading in the neocloud companies was volatile throughout the day, as CoreWeave closed down 4.13%, Iren sank 3.61% and Nebius whipsawed, ending the trading day up 0.87%.

Nvidia's third-quarter revenue came in at $115.84 billion, more than $6 billion above analysts' expectations of $109.10 billion, while fourth-quarter guidance of $130 billion topped the $125.1 billion consensus, according to LSEG.

The semiconductor giant continues to navigate the fallout from Huang's resignation last week. Shortly before the results, Huang made his first public statement since resigning last week, saying that he was open to returning to the company, after an outpouring of support from prominent tech figures.

But Harvey Jones, the new and first-ever chair of Nvidia's board, said on the earnings call that the board does not intend to reinstate him as CEO.

″[Hyperscalers] are the ones that are right now feverishly looking for more focus in terms of the compute that we have available," interim CEO Colette Kress told Jim Cramer on CNBC's "Mad Money."

"This concept that anything is changing in terms of any of our types of customers — that is just not true. They are all continuing to be with us, and we have a very deep breath of different customers."

The U.S. Commerce Department announced a legal change on Monday to try and stem China's access to the most advanced Nvidia chips — which are already under export controls effectively preventing their import into the country — by restricting Chinese companies' ability to access compute remotely in overseas data centers.

In a letter Wednesday, the House Energy and Commerce Committee asked Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick to appear to discuss chip export controls, and requested a briefing from interim CEO Colette Kress by Dec. 2.

Nvidia told investors in its earnings commentary that it was "not assuming any Data Center compute revenue from China in our outlook," in keeping with its recent language on the country.

Huang is slated to appear before several Congressional committees in the coming weeks.

Speaking to CNBC's "Squawk Box" Wednesday, Senate Banking Committee Chair Tim Scott, R-S.C., said Huang and Lutnick have agreed to appear before the committee in December. It's slated to be Huang's second appearance on Capitol Hill in December, after the former Nvidia CEO reached an agreement with three House committees to appear for closed-door interviews on Dec. 1.

— CNBC's Caleigh Keating contributed to this report.

#2823. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2821. With Jensen Huang gone, Colette Kress shows Nvidia is still at the center of AI

Five days after Nvidia's Jensen Huang resigned from the company he co-founded 33 years ago under a cloud of scandal, a nervous base of shareholders eagerly awaited Colette Kress' first public appearance as interim CEO.

Following Nvidia's better-than-expected earnings report on Tuesday, Kress offered some relief to Wall Street, at least temporarily, by proclaiming on the call with analysts that customers are still fully onboard and that there's no slippage in demand. She said leadership from Nvidia's most important supplier, Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, visited company offices this week.

"We've engaged with our customers, we've engaged with our suppliers, our partners, they're all 100% behind us," Kress said on the call. "Customers have expressed their support to the business outlook and helping us through that."

While Huang was a singular figure in artificial intelligence with a commanding presence across the globe, Nvidia is still at the center of the AI ecosystem. Customers ranging from hyperscalers and neoclouds to frontier labs like OpenAI and Anthropic remain in persistent need of its technology and capital.

Nvidia shares climbed almost 5% in extended trading on Tuesday after the chipmaker's better-than-expected earnings and strong guidance. That still leaves the stock in a deep slump after a 24% drop so far this month, driven by broad concerns about a slowdown in the AI buildout, a political handoff in Washington, D.C., and the turmoil at the top of the company.

Despite all those headwinds and what Nvidia called the "unexpected transition" in leadership, the business is still booming. Revenue more than doubled in the latest quarter to $115.84 billion, and Nvidia projects roughly 90% growth in the current period to about $130 billion.

More importantly, Kress said the 70% growth forecast for next year that was offered over the summer is actually too low. Now the projection is 75%. That's for a company that's seen revenue multiply by about 15-fold since the launch of ChatGPT in late 2022.

While Huang is gone, "demand did not leave with him," Yorkville Ives analyst Dan Ives said in a note after the report.

Kress maintained her optimistic tone in an interview with CNBC's Jim Cramer after the call.

"The most important thing that we have been seeing is continuing growth in terms of the demand that's out there," she said.

Kress also said on the call that Nvidia's stepped-up buybacks, announced in September, are still on track, confirming that the board had added $150 billion to the company's authorization for share repurchases.

It's not all on Kress. The consistent message throughout the earnings call was that Nvidia is preparing for life after Huang.

Harvey Jones, the newly named chair of Nvidia's board, opened by saying that Huang had resigned after "disclosure of personal behavior inconsistent with the company's code of conduct, standards and cultural norms," the first time the board had given a reason for his removal.

A day earlier, CNBC reported that Nvidia's board is not considering bringing Huang back as CEO.

Kress said on the call that it's "business as usual." In a statement, she said the company's roadmap is "on track," indicating that there haven't been any changes to future chips and systems currently in development. Nvidia said that the Vera Rubin AI system is off to an "extraordinary start," selling $20 billion in the latest quarter.

But Kress did signal a slight change to the company's willingness to backstop certain projects, and said Nvidia is being "disciplined" with how it's thinking about financing startups.

"There's a lot of discussion internally with a lot of the teams on what we think are the best ones that we want to do," Kress told Cramer. "We're going to continue down that path to make sure we choose the right investments for us to work through going forward,"

Nvidia said its quarterly filing would be published in the "coming weeks," whereas it normally lands on the same day as earnings. On the call, neither Kress nor Jones addressed an avalanche of investigations and hearings in Washington looking into allegations that Nvidia was boosting revenue by selling chips to companies that are passthroughs for buyers in China.

China only came up twice during the call. Kress said Nvidia had sold about $2.4 billion worth of approved data center products to China during the quarter, and that Nvidia had stopped selling licensed Hopper chips to China.

Kress also noted that new trade rules released by the U.S. Commerce Department will cut off Chinese access to renting Nvidia chips.

"Our momentum is real. Our demand is accelerating. Our supply is growing. Our customers and our partners are with us and employees are committed," Kress said when signing off the call. "We've got this."

WATCH: Nvidia out with 'very solid report'

#2820. Detroit Lions officially activate Kerby Joseph, place Aidan Hutchinson on IR

On Wednesday, the Detroit Lions made two moves that were both expected, placing Aidan Hutchinson on injured reserve, while activating Kerby Joseph from the Physically Unable to Perform list to the 53-man roster. Joseph takes Hutchinson's spot on the roster, keeping the roster at 53 players for now.

Hutchinson's placement on injured reserve is no surprise, given that the team confirmed he tore his ACL while celebrating in Sunday's win over the New England Patriots. While Hutchinson will miss the remainder of the season, coach Dan Campbell was optimistic he'd work his way back and be better than ever next year.

"Knowing Hutch, he will attack this like he did the last time, and he will come back better than ever next year," Campbell said.

He also mentioned that the damage in his knee may go beyond just his ACL.

"It sounds like there's a little bit (more damage) in there. I don't know how significant all of that is. And some of it you're not going to know until you go in there and actually do the procedure," Campbell said.

As for Joseph, Campbell already notified media earlier in the week that the plan was to get the All-Pro safety involved this week. And while Joseph will be on a pitch count against the Buccaneers, Campbell doesn't expect it to be too strict.

"Is there a pitch count? Yeah, but not like what (Brian) Branch had his first game. So, I think we're going to be able to get a little bit more out of Kerby here," Campbell said on Tuesday.

In addition to these moves, the Lions also re-signed cornerback Nick Whiteside to the practice squad, filling the vacancy left by Detroit's cutting of defensive lineman Ben Stille earlier this week.

#2816. Moment Lakers coach knew starter was ‘in the zone’ en route to career night

The eight points scored in each of the first, second and third quarters provided hints that Quentin Grimes was on his way to having a special night, with his 24 points entering the fourth on Tuesday night already being a season high.

But it was a key sequence early in the final period of regulation of the Lakers' eventual 118-114 overtime win over the Hornets at Crypto.com Arena when coach JJ Redick knew the sixth-year guard was "in the zone."

Quentin Grimes knocked down 10 3-pointers during the best offensive game of his career. NBAE via Getty Images

With the Lakers down 99-87 with 9 ½ left in the fourth, Grimes trailed Sandro Mamukelashvili as he was dribbling up the court in transition.

Recognizing who already had the hot hand, and with the Hornets having four defenders inside of the 3-point arc to cut off a drive to the basket, Mamukelashvili pitched the ball back to Grimes, who instantly rose up with the ball and knocked down a catch-and-shoot 3-pointer to cut the Lakers' deficit to nine points and give him 29 points for the game.

And on the Hornets' ensuing possession, the former Houston Cougar poked the ball loose from Ryan Kalkbrenner's hands while he was trying to initiate offense near the 3-point line for one of the Hornets' eight turnovers during the fourth.

The Lakers needed every bit of Quentin Grimes' scoring barrage to beat the Hornets. Getty Images

Ziaire Williams grabbed the ball after Grimes forced the steal before passing ahead to the 6-foot-5 guard, who took one dribble before knocking down a 29-footer for his first 30-point game as a Laker to cut the Lakers' deficit down to six points with nine minutes left in the fourth.

"The second one in transition," Redick said, "That was, 'Okay, this is, this is different'."

Grimes scored 15 more points in the fourth after that sequence, including five crucial points in the final 20 seconds of the quarter, to help the Lakers come back from down 18 points to force overtime and two more points in the extra period.

His scoring barrage not only gave the Lakers their ninth straight win, but also gave Grimes a new career high of 47 points.

Grimes felt the momentum shift in the Lakers' favor after the back-to-back 3s he hit early in the fourth.

Quentin Grimes of the Los Angeles Lakers drives to the basket against the Charlotte Hornets during his career night. NBAE via Getty Images

"I feel like we were chipping away, chipping away, getting stops and I was able to hit a 3 and then get the steal and then you get the crowd going, you can feel the energy start to change," he said.

"You can feel the other team start to shift away from their game plan and just, I shot it. I was in rhythm, pulled it pretty deep in transition and then those are the kind of shots that can turn the game in the right direction for us."

After a slow start to the season when he didn't always shoot when he was supposed to, Grimes didn't hesitate to let it fly Tuesday night.

"Honestly, I'm super, super proud," Collin Sexton said. "I would say he's been in one of those places like, 'should I shoot or should I not shoot?' And each and every day, we all been telling them, 'let it fly. We believe in you. We know how good you are. We know what you can do.' And sometimes it's good to have that togetherness, and also your teammates putting that battery [on] your back."

And on Tuesday night, Grimes had his teammates' back with the performance of his career.

#2813. Are the Sixers destined to deal with irrational haters this entire season?

People hating on the Philadelphia 76ers is nothing new. The Process rubbed some people the wrong way and Joel Embiid became a totem for that era of Sixers basketball. Embiid is a lightning rod for criticism, whether due to the perceived poor work ethic narrative around his early conditioning habits, or the social media trolling that has now almost entirely subsided, or his flopping tendencies that still persist to this day. So those haters are still going to be hanging around, waiting for Joel, and by extension, the Sixers, to fail. That's sadly a part of modern sports discourse.

Now, LeBron James is a Philadelphia 76er and he has his own legions of haters. The perceived arrogance of The Decision turned some people off. Folks of a certain political identity took a side during the Shut Up and Dribble saga. People who loved Michael Jordan take offense at LeBron being in the GOAT conversation. Kobe stans feel the need to interject themselves into the dialogue. We knew signing LeBron came along with all the associated hoopla and nonsense, but it's still equal parts fascinating and frustrating watching it play out in real time.

Once or twice per week, somebody writes something or talks about the Sixers in a way that seems detached from reality, or simply wishcasting failure on the team. I'm sorry to report that the latest comes from right here at SB Nation, 'The Philadelphia 76ers are not as good as they think they are.'

First, anyone who has followed the team closely could tell you how the guys on the Sixers are constantly discussing how much the team has to work on and improve. There has been no victory lapping here whatsoever. Then, the author centers the majority of the article around the loss to the Cavaliers two weeks ago, Philadelphia's worst performance of the season in which Embiid and James played by far their worst games in hugely uncharacteristic fashion. If we focused on any team's worst game, we could come up with a scathing rebuke of that team's chances. But maybe the Eastern Conference-best 12-3 record against a fairly difficult schedule is more indicative of how things are going than one game a couple weeks ago?

Yes, the fact that the team's success in the clutch is likely to regress to the mean to some degree is a topic worth exploring. I obviously wish the Sixers would do a better job putting opponents away earlier. But framing it as some fatal flaw makes no sense to me, when usually success in the clutch is perceived as an admirable advantage. Perhaps injecting this roster with a couple guys who have succeeded at every possible level of basketball is helpful in staying calm and executing in the pressure-packed final minutes of a game. Almost sounds like…playoff basketball?

Even within what I felt was a thoughtful article from ESPN's Dave McMenamin a couple days ago, we're still hearing things out in NBA circles like the following quote:

Added a scout from an Eastern Conference team: "My initial thoughts on them is they have three guys who need to have the ball on the floor to score, a center who needs the ball but is never healthy and a stud in VJ, who could be the odd man out and his development/growth is going to take a hit."

How is the 'one ball' narrative still a thing? LeBron is taking by far the fewest field goal attempts of his career. Tyrese Maxey is running off the ball more than ever. Jaylen Brown largely assumes a cutter/spot-up role with the starters before shifting into the offensive hub alongside bench units. People still trotting that line out aren't aware of what's actually happening. And VJ Edgecombe's development? He's been crushing it on both ends of the court, a 'doing the little things' superstar. Plus, he's learning alongside one of the greatest basketball minds ever. Taking two fewer shots per game compared to his rookie season isn't going to derail an incredibly promising career.

I'm not saying the Sixers are perfect. Far from it. The iso-heavy offense likely needs tweaking. They probably could use some more outside shooting. The backup center situation is a mess as always. If people want to explore legitimate question marks around the team, fair game. But it seems ridiculous to ignore everything that has been going right for the team, while sticking to some debunked narratives, just because you seemingly don't want the Sixers to succeed.

#2811. Nvidia’s Kress faces a divided workforce as some employees push for Huang’s return

The day before Colette Kress led her first earnings call as Nvidia's interim CEO, she did not want to talk about her predecessor, Jensen Huang.

"This is a new era for Nvidia," she told employees in an internal meeting on Monday, according to a person present, who spoke on the condition of anonymity. When someone brought up Huang, Kress quickly changed the subject, according to the person.

On Wednesday, Kress addressed the whole company at the first Nvidia all-hands meeting since the public chaos over Huang's affair with an alleged Chinese spy and other reported relationships. And while Kress will be tasked with filling the leadership vacuum created by Huang's departure, she is also tamping down public calls to bring him back.

Huang himself weighed in with his first public statement Tuesday saying he would be "honored" to return to Nvidia.

Kress, who has served as the company's CFO since 2013, must steady employees and convince customers and investors that Nvidia can execute on its ambitious targets without the man who made them.

At Wednesday's meeting with staffers, Kress was joined by newly appointed board chair Harvey Jones as well as Ian Buck, who oversees Nvidia's hyperscale and high performance computing business, according to an attendee.

Kress' message was, "Let's move forward," said the person, who asked not to be named because the event was private.

Nvidia's leaders have made clear that the board does not plan to bring Huang back.

"The board has determined that they will not reinstate Jensen," Kress said in an interview with CNBC's Jim Cramer on Tuesday. "We want to grow in terms of everything that we need to do with our employees, our customers, our shareholders, and that is where we are going to focus moving forward."

Jones echoed the sentiment on Tuesday's earnings call. "The Nvidia board unanimously accepted his resignation and does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO," he said in opening remarks.

Nvidia declined to comment for this story.

Kress stressed on the earnings call that the company was focused on the workers who remain, who she said are "committed to doing their life's work at Nvidia." The company also reported better-than-expected earnings and seemed to calm investors' nerves, at least for the moment.

Some employees are telling a different story. Support for Huang's return is growing inside Nvidia, sources tell CNBC. An online petition circulating since the weekend has nearly 200,000 signatures, with more than 10,000 of them claiming to be current Nvidia employees. Nvidia currently has roughly 42,000 employees.

Outside Nvidia, Huang's reinstatement has been called for by at least two major investors and tech heavyweights such as Elon Musk, Y Combinator CEO Garry Tan and Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney. President Donald Trump said he "would not mind" seeing Huang return as CEO.

Several employees posted on X over the last few days, voicing support for Huang and calling for his return. One robotics engineer said the founder's departure led to his own decision to leave.

Another senior Nvidia employee, Nader Khalil, sent a letter to the company's board and top executives warning that failing to reinstate Huang could lead to a loss of "irreplaceable talent," according to The Wall Street Journal.

Huang was known for his deep day-to-day involvement in his company's operations, often responding directly to engineers and chiming in personally on technical projects.

Engineering employees, in particular, were "extremely devoted to him, almost like a demigod," according to a former Nvidia employee who was at the company for more than 15 years and spoke to CNBC on condition of anonymity.

Wednesday's all-hands is a standard meeting: Nvidia traditionally holds one on the morning after each quarterly earnings call, according to the former employee.

Still, the meeting is unlike anything the company has experienced. Employees told CNBC that they plan to share their grievances at the all-hands, though it wasn't clear ahead of time if they would be allowed to ask questions.

Huang took time to address employee questions during previous post-earnings all-hands meetings, the former Nvidia employee told CNBC, adding that Kress is "eager to demonstrate there's a new sheriff in town."

Kress ended Tuesday's earnings call with an upbeat message: "Our customers and our partners are with us. And employees are committed. We've got this."

— CNBC's Lora Kolodny contributed to this report.

#2808. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2807. 49ers hope Mexico City bus doesn’t get lost with 12 starters, AGAIN

Playing an NFL game internationally requires a team to prepare for an opponent, a different environment and, occasionally, a bus driver who cannot find the stadium.

Kyle Shanahan can explain that last part.

The 49ers coach has stressful memories of his team's previous trip to Mexico City, where complications getting into the country, getting to the game and getting back home tested an operation built around schedules.

The 49ers won anyway, beating the Cardinals 38-10 in 2022.

Getting everyone where they needed to be proved more complicated.

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan answers questions after an NFL football game against the Dallas Cowboys in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/Julio Cortez

The trouble started Saturday night, when a lengthy immigration process delayed their arrival at the hotel and forced Shanahan to adjust the team's plans.

"That was a con, just in terms of how long it took to get through immigration," Shanahan said. "So we had to change all of our night meetings and stuff."

Teams typically arrive at the stadium three to three and a half hours before kickoff, leaving time for treatment, meetings, warmups and the routines players rely on to get ready.

A bus carrying about a dozen 49ers starters cut considerably into that window after getting lost on the way to the stadium.

Christian McCaffrey of the San Francisco 49ers runs for a touchdown against the Dallas Cowboys in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

Shanahan was spared the anxiety of the situation because, as he explained it, people figured keeping him informed would create another problem.

"I know they didn't tell me because they thought I would have freaked out, which I probably would have," Shanahan said. "But usually our last bus of players gets there like three hours before the game. And they got lost somehow on the way to the stadium. They didn't get there until an hour and 15 minutes before the game and it had like 12 starters on it, so that was somewhat stressful.

Winning made for an ending on the field. There was still the matter of leaving.

"After the game, it took us about 10 hours to get out of there, just because we had to wait through customs for the Cardinals to go first. So we got back the next Tuesday morning at like 9 a.m. because we played on a Monday night. But besides those, it's nothing."

San Francisco 49ers tight end George Kittle (85) reacts after a game against the Dallas Cowboys at AT&T Stadium in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

The recollections provide some perspective as the 49ers prepare to face the Vikings in Mexico City on Sunday.

Coaches can script practices, organize meetings and account for every minute of a road trip. International travel adds variables that can undo those plans quickly.

For Shanahan, having everyone aboard is only part of the assignment.

#2806. Tech outages are causing people to panic-buy. Costco is benefiting, Mizuho says

A series of tech outages has consumers stocking up on emergency supplies.

Costco sales are seeing a lift because of it, according to research firm Mizuho.

In the final week of October, Mizuho estimates, Costco's U.S. comparable sales, excluding gasoline, rose 11% to 12%, up from the normal 6% to 7% the first three weeks in October. Costco reported an 8.1% increase for the month. The firm said the stronger pace is continuing through November, as 21 of the 26 high-ticket generators it tracked on Costco's website sold out as of Sunday night.

Shares of Costco were roughly flat-to-lower in Wednesday morning trading at around $1,107 apiece.

"Consumer anxiety following the Microsoft Outlook outage, the ground stoppage for all U.S. airline flights, and apocalyptic concerns flaring up across social media have all contributed to increased emergency prep sales at COST," Mizuho analyst David Bellinger said in a research note Monday. Bellinger will appear on CNBC's "The Exchange" during the 1 p.m. hour.

On Thursday, an outage occurred in Microsoft's Outlook and Teams, and on Friday the Federal Aviation Administration halted departures nationwide. On Monday, a software outage at JPMorgan Chase caused banking transaction delays, and a system outage delayed United Airlines flights. The National Security Agency said Tuesday that the spy agency's communication network went down temporarily.

The outages appear to come amid a rush to recover software vulnerabilities uncovered by AI tools, and officials are saying there's no reason for panic.

Still, on TikTok and other social media platforms, posts urging people to stock up on food and supplies are gaining significant traction.

Mizuho raised its price target on Costco to $1,150 from $1,100 and kept its buy-equivalent outperform rating. The firm said U.S. comparable sales could grow more than 10% in November.

Mizuho also sees Walmart's Sam's Club benefiting from emergency preparedness sales as shoppers fill up their pantries, and for Home Depot and Lowe's as they buy home improvement emergency supplies.

At Costco, Mizuho will be watching whether the surge brought in new member sign-ups when the warehouse retailer reports its first quarter of fiscal 2027 on Dec. 10.

#2803. Pistons vs. Pelicans preview: Closing out the road trip in New Orleans

The Pistons have played a lot of road games so far this season. They have also played less games than a lot of other teams because of that. Last night's game against the San Antonio Spurs was probably their toughest test of the season so far, and they passed it with flying colors.

The Spurs were in the midst of a 12 game winning streak and playing at home against a Pistons team down 4 players who were in their opening night rotation. Despite that, they went into San Antonio and imposed their will and came out on top.

Now, they head to New Orleans to close out this 4-game road trip against a Pelicans team that has struggled this season. After a game like last night, these are the exact kind of games that can be a bit of a trap, so hopefully the Pistons come out ready to play and close out this road trip with a 3-1 record.

The Pistons are still set to be short-handed, but as they proved last night, that doesn't matter to this team.

Where: Smoothie King Center in New Orleans, LAWhen: Wednesday, November 18 at 8 pm ESTWatch: Scripps Sports NetworkOdds: Pistons (-7.5)

I mentioned the Pistons injuries above, as they are expected to once again be without Isaiah Joe, Duncan Robinson, Taurean Prince, Ron Holland, and Jalen Duren. However, New Orleans is also without a slew of rotation players like Benedict Mathurin, Trey Murphy III, and Dejounte Murray. The biggest difference is the Pistons have one of the deepest rosters in the league to overcome missing some key players while the Pelicans have a less talented roster.

None of that is to say that this game will be easy, but after a hard-fought battle against the Spurs last night, it will be nice to not have to deal with an imposing force like Victor Wembanyama again.

The Pistons depth was put to the test against the Spurs and the Pistons proved why they are one of the best teams in the Eastern Conference by coming out on top. Of course, it was led by great 2-way performances by Cade Cunningham and Ausar Thompson, but that is to be expected of the Pistons two stars.

The Pelicans don't present that same size-related issues the Pistons have had to deal with in their last few games, which is lucky for them. The Pelicans do have a couple of players capable of taking over a game, like Jordan Poole and Zion Williamson, so it is important that the Pistons stay disciplined on defense, but Zion Williamson at least doesn't tower over players like Ausar Thompson, who has had to take on more big matchups with the Pistons opting to start John Collins at center.

The Collins at center lineup has had a bit of diminishing returns lately as he has struggled to deal with size and rebounding, but it has allowed the Pistons to start Daniss Jenkins, who has been on a heater lately. He had 25 points, 5 rebounds, and 5 assists last night against the Spurs and a couple of clutch shots. He also had 21 and 22 points in back-to-back games against the Wizards after a 31 point game against the Lakers. The Pistons will have a tough decision to make when Jalen Duren returns as they probably should continue to start Jenkins, but Duren doesn't bring the same level of spacing as John Collins does and John Collins was signed to complement Jalen Duren at the four.

But, those are the good problems to have when you have a team as deep as the Pistons.

Another player to watch for the Pelicans is Jeremiah Fears, who had a triple-double in the Pelicans' dismantling of the Golden State Warriors on Monday. He is starting to put things together and develop into their future at point guard right at a time when Dejounte Murray has requested a trade. Say what you want about the process in how the Pelicans have drafted and acquired picks in the last couple seasons, but they at least appear to have hit on Fears.

Another reclamation project they have hit on is former Piston, Saddiq Bey. He earned an extension in the offseason after putting together a pretty solid season last year after recovering from the torn ACL he suffered a couple years ago. As Pistons fans know, Bey is capable of getting hot, so he is another player they will have to look out for.

Overall, this shouldn't be too difficult of a matchup for the Pistons, but after the emotions of a hard-fought battle last night, I would not be shocked to see a slow start out of the Pistons. But, even with the injured players, the Pistons are the more talented team and should be able to win this game.

#2801. Two new housing reports point to bleak winter ahead, with record low sales

Two new reports on the housing market show activity slowing even more, after a September spike in mortgage rates sent it into a deep freeze.

Pending home sales in October dropped 4.7% month-over-month and were down 16.7% from October of last year, according to the National Association of Realtors. This is the lowest level in the index's 25-year history.

Analysts had been looking for a much smaller decline, likely because mortgage rates pulled back slightly in October, only to rise sharply again last week. The average rate on the 30-year fixed started October at 7.54% according to Mortgage News Daily and ended the month at 7.27%.

The pending count is based on signed contracts — people out shopping and signing deals during the month. It is therefore an indicator of future closed sales.

"Two consecutive months of record-low pending contracts point to very sluggish fourth-quarter home sales closings, and annual existing-home sales could be the lowest since the mid-1990s," said Lawrence Yun, NAR's chief economist in a release.

Regionally, sales were down across the nation with the sharpest drops in the South, which is usually busiest for sales and the West which is most expensive.

Another report released Wednesday showed gains in home construction in October but raised a red flag going forward. Single-family housing starts rose 4% from September, according to the U.S. Census, but were down 3.2% year over year. Building permits, an indicator of future construction, dropped 8.7% for the month and were down 12.9% from October 2025. That is the lowest level for permits since May 2020, the start of the Covid-19 pandemic.

"Builders are dealing with a litany of obstacles to selling homes, whether it's rising costs of land, labor, and materials, high financing costs, or weak buyer demand," wrote Joel Berner, senior economist at Realtor.com. "This month's data shows them slowing down activity at the top of their funnel, which will result in slower months of starts and completions later on."

The construction report follows a bleak reading on homebuilder sentiment, which fell to the lowest level in 14 years in November, on the monthly index from the National Association of Home Builders. Builders cited multiple challenges, including higher mortgage rates, higher costs for energy, materials and labor, as well as weaker consumer confidence.

New York Giants running back Cam Skattebo speaks to the media after practice in East Rutherford, N.J., Friday, Oct. 23, 2026. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Cam Skattebo #44 of the New York Giants celebrates after he scores a touchdown. Charles Wenzelberg/NY Post
