#2798. Former Lakota West guard Josh Tyson officially signs with UC Bearcats

Jerrod Calhoun officially has another local recruit coming his way for 2027-28 as former Lakota West guard Josh Tyson signed with the Cincinnati Bearcats Nov. 18 from his current school, La Lumiere in La Porte, Indiana.

The 6-foot-2, 180-pound four-star guard with the 6-foot-10 wingspan posted a photo of the ceremony on Instagram.

More: UC Bearcats get Jerrod Calhoun his 300th career win, beating Dayton

Tyson transferred to the Indiana school in early July after averaging 16.3 points, 3.8 assists and two steals per game while shooting nearly 63% last season for coach Kelven Moss at Lakota West. Moss was Coach of the Year in the Greater Miami Conference and Tyson was GMC Player of the Year.

Tyson shot 34.4% from the perimeter as the Firebirds went 24-2. His top game of 28 points came against his current school, La Lumiere, last season in a 72-71 defeat.

He committed to Jerrod Calhoun and the Bearcats less than two months ago, coming back to Lakota West for the ceremony.

"Being from Cincinnati, it was an easy choice," Tyson said. "It (Calhoun's system) is positionless, and it's flow. It's very similar to (Lakota West) and La Lumiere's (current school) offense. I thrive in the split-action, and it's super easy."

Current Princeton High School guard Kam Mercer, who also just signed with Calhoun and the Bearcats, attended the ceremony for Moss in late September.

"I just wanted to play with him," Mercer said. "Most people don't get the opportunity, but it's two of us at one school, and it's in our backyard. We're looking forward to turning Bearcat Nation up!"

#2793. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2792. Read Jim Cramer’s full interview with Nvidia’s Colette Kress: Demand, buybacks and China

Nvidia delivered another beat-and-raise quarter on Tuesday in the chipmaker's first report since Jensen Huang abruptly resigned as CEO last week amid a widening affair scandal. Revenue more than doubled from a year earlier, exceeding estimates by nearly $7 billion, while earnings topped expectations by 20 cents per share. An excellent quarter by almost any measure.

But investors remain skeptical about Nvidia's future without Huang, who famously founded the company at a San Jose Denny's in 1993. Shares fell 2.5% Wednesday, despite opening higher on the session. Will the company lose its smartest executives and engineers? Would Nvidia remain banker to the increasingly shaky neoclouds? Can it stay ahead of the competition?

On Tuesday night, Colette Kress sat down with CNBC's Jim Cramer shortly after delivering her first earnings call as interim CEO to answer these pressing questions and more.

This transcript has been edited for clarity.

Jim Cramer: After the bell, Nvidia reported earnings, and what a quarter it was. The stock jumped in after hours in response. This is the first time we are hearing from the company since its founder, Jensen Huang, stepped down as CEO last week. So let's check in with Colette Kress, Nvidia's longtime chief financial officer and now its interim CEO. Ms. Kress, welcome to "Mad Money."

Colette Kress: Thank you for having me, Jim. It's really great to see you.

Cramer: Thank you, Colette. The most important thing I heard tonight was that you increased your revenue growth forecast for next year. Previously, you said you would grow 70%. Now you're saying 75%, off of a higher base. How are you able to do that?

Kress: Thanks. Very, very important to understand how much our customers are interested in our compute infrastructure and how much they need the agentic solutions out there. So the most important thing that we have been seeing is just, again, a continuous growth in terms of the demand that's out there. But we also have been working in terms of our supply. So an increase in supply, working with all of our different suppliers, whether that be focusing in terms of what we need in terms of chips, what we need in terms of advanced packaging or memory, all of that is also coming together. We felt very confident, therefore, that we could establish that we will grow 75% year over year for fiscal year '28.

Cramer: Now, a lot of people have been saying that there was one man at this company. I've talked with you. There are another 49,000. How is the team holding up? Are there people who are saying, "You know what, this is not the Nvidia I know, I don't want to be there?" Is there a brain drain, as some people have told me?

Kress: Yeah, really great question. And remember, the company has been built on the perspective that employees see Nvidia as their life's work. And many of them have been here for decades, working with us from both a technology and a business-and-operations perspective. And now where we're standing is focusing on the business, focusing on getting all of that work done. And I had been working already with leadership. Leadership is supportive of everything we're doing and is standing up to help as we pull all employees and leaders together to move forward. But we have high confidence that our employees are definitely still working, still working with us, and enjoy really the work here at Nvidia.

Cramer: I have talked to you many times about the idea that the cheapest stock in the world is yours, and that's why you don't necessarily have to help other people buy your chips when you can just go buy your own stock. Where are you on buybacks versus lending aggressive amounts of money to, say, neoclouds? Because I think that the buybacks are a lot better for shareholders.

Kress: We have now been looking at the capability of making significant stock repurchases for our company. That is enabled by our significant growth and the cash we will have. During the quarter, we discussed increasing the amount of additional stock repurchases to more than $150 billion. That adds to a total amount at this point of $277 billion that we're going to be able to provide. We are completely committed to that. We do believe that that's something we're going to be able to complete all the way through, just to the end of 2027. So we are in line and continuing our stock repurchases for sure.

Cramer: OK. Thank you. That's important. And it's more aggressive than I thought. And I'm glad to hear that. Now, why are there many different reports about your company, and even about your accounting? I know you as the person who is in charge of the numbers. For instance, I read two different analysts who say you may be on the hook for about $530 billion in guarantees, which is worrisome because it could have a huge impact on your cash flow.  I heard a new disclosure tonight that made me feel like the on-the-hook number is not true.

Kress: Right now, our guarantees have been thoughtfully completed to date. And our guarantees are approximately about $118 billion, which includes one of the most important ones: a land, power, and shell backstop that we have with Portsmouth here in the United States, the best site for what we see, the future of multiple, multiple buildings working together to do that, about 4.5 gigawatts to do so. That is solely for Nvidia's infrastructure. It's working very well and is getting ready for all the builds it needs to do. And that is the most significant part of our guarantees. We will continue to do the best due diligence and thought regarding any additional guarantees that need to be done. That means we're going to be working with companies to make sure we understand where they are in terms of their land, power, shell builds, how they are doing in terms of getting ready for the compute, getting the capital that they need, and making sure that they have the end customer, the off-take that we'll need to complete that. So it's going to be a top priority. But we are all working together to make sure that we can support the most important ones that we think are running very well, and that's going to be a top priority for us.

Cramer: Speaking of priorities, from a lot of reporting, again, because I want to try to get all this stuff out. You were kind enough to come on and speak about anything unrehearsed. Based on much of the reporting over the last two weeks, it seems that the aggressive investments in the ecosystem, including some companies that are less than investment-grade, were a priority for your predecessor and his family members. I want to know if you were pushing back on those? And should we see less and expect less support for the ecosystem and more for the shareholders?

Kress: Our investments are an important part of the work we can do, focusing on a set of organizations that may be AI-native, maybe new types of companies starting, as well as helping those that want to build new types of models in the market. These are some of the areas that we focused on. But, yes, we're very disciplined in terms of how we think that through. There's a lot of discussion internally with a lot of the teams on what we think are the best ones that we want to do. And we're going to continue down that path to make sure we choose the right investments for us to work through going forward. But most importantly, these are companies in areas where we can see additional growth in AI. And I think those are important for us. But, again, stepping back to our business and how important our capital is going to be for procuring supply and getting ready for all of our employees as well, and all the work that we need to do from the engineering, that's going to be our top priority in terms of our use of capital to do so. So that's what I think is the most important.

Cramer: You are in a very competitive situation. For instance, AMD has a product that's a GPU that is much touted, and they think it can be moved in quicker. I know that some of the hyperscalers are very good at developing their own chips, which makes some people say, "Wait a second. Is what's really happening that Nvidia is supporting some companies that may not be, let's say, investment-grade or the kind of customer you would want — neoclouds — because it's losing business from the traditional hyperscalers and their shares are going down. Is that true?

Kress: Interesting statement, but no, it is not true. If we want to think about where we are with our hyperscalers, they are right now feverishly looking for more focus on the compute we have available. I have seen already with many of these contacting me and saying, " Great to see everything intact. Great to see that there's more supply. I need more demand." So they are all with us. And remember, there's a reason why. That is an amazing type of architecture that has been built through so much work with the different types of engineers. It is the most durable, fungible, and performant type of infrastructure out there. The idea that anything is changing among our customer types is just not true. They are all still with us. And we have a very deep breadth of different customers. And I think that's important to understand. These will be needed for all the different organizations overseas as well. They're going to want to say, "How do I have someone building a different type of data center overseas that they would want to see as well?" So I do believe it is appropriate to have different types of organizations that will be using this type of compute. But keep in mind, the stock, the amount of different types of customers, is broad. And we are going to continue to focus on that.

Cramer: This level of demand makes me ask you a particular question, which is that, if you were no longer allowed or wanted to ship into Southeast Asia for fear that it might end up in China, the flow back would not overwhelm pricing and drive it down, but instead would just help sate some of demand, but not all the demand? You do not need Southeast Asia to make or advance your numbers?

Kress: Well, what we are always working on is: Where do we feel we can provide the demand to our customers? We are always very, very cautious in terms of where we will be shipping to and making sure, yes, the data center is ready, and whether or not we also know that off-take and we know that customer piece of it. That is part of our process, and we will continue there. Let's just remember what we are right now in terms of our shipments to China. Our shipments to China are approximately about 10%. But that is our gaming business. That is our automotive business. That is our workstation business that is going there. As you also saw us announce, we would only have the licensed H200s [Nvidia's data center GPUs], and those went to China within this last quarter. And we are no longer shipping H200 at all, let alone not shipping it to China. So we are very careful and will continue to focus on that.

Cramer: I do want to ask you something that I think was a question that was handled by the board, but I didn't necessarily get an answer that I felt satisfactory. I understand, and have heard, that the chairman of the board says the board does not intend to ask Jensen Huang to return as CEO. But I have to say that perhaps the board would consider, after a thorough investigation — which I do not believe they did, frankly — that Jensen's value to our nation's technology, as expressed by our president today, could lead him to do something else for you. Perhaps a chief technologist or an ambassador for Nvidia's dominance who would not run afoul of either the SEC or anything done by Commerce or the Secretary of the Treasury.

Kress: Jensen resigned from his position with Nvidia, and the board accepted his resignation. The board has determined that they will not reinstate Jensen. And that is our statement and what the board has decided to do. We want to think about growing our business right now. We want to grow in everything we do with our employees, our customers, and our shareholders, and that is where we will focus moving forward.

Cramer: Do you think that it's possible that the two-way compute market project that Jensen started, that was going to be done with Goldman, with BlackRock, with Apollo, will continue? That's $500 billion in financing to make it happen the way we think it should.

Kress: Those financing funds are very important to us. We announced that they will continue to work with us, and their continued assistance with many of these companies that may be just shy of investment-grade and want to explore financing options; we're excited that they are part of this work. They are part of helping us create an asset class of our systems as well. No other company has that durability and fungibility outside of Nvidia. So they are important partners. We're going to continue working to help many of the different companies get the financing that they may want to do and the efficiency of that financing.

Cramer: You cleared up many questions for us tonight, and I really appreciate it. I know there are a lot of people who, I think, say a lot of things about your company that aren't true. I want to thank you, Colette Kress, who's been true north for me for many, many years, for as long as we've known each other. I want to say — I would say congratulations on the interim CEO. I'm glad you were on our show tonight, and I hope those who want to understand what kind of company this really is go back over what she said. It's quite different from what I hear every day on Wall Street. Colette Kress is the former CFO, and interim CEO, and it was great to have you on the show. Thank you, Colette.

#2791. 13 Game Review: Suns by the Numbers

The Phoenix Suns just finished the second night of a back-to-back with a road win in Denver, and they sit at 6-7 through 13 games. We're officially 15.85% of the way through the 2026-27 season. Time is flying!

It's been a rollercoaster start. The night before Denver, the Lakers came into the Mortgage Matchup Center and handed Phoenix a loss. Twenty-four hours later, the Suns went into altitude on tired legs and got their first road win of the season. That's the season in a nutshell… a team that is capable of scoring with anyone but keeps giving away possessions on the other end.

PHOENIX, ARIZONA – NOVEMBER 07: Head coach Jordan Ott of the Phoenix Suns gestures during the first half of the game against the Houston Rockets at Mortgage Matchup Center on November 07, 2026 in Phoenix, Arizona. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Chris Coduto/Getty Images) | Getty Images

Seed: 11th in West (half a game behind Dallas for 10th, 1.5 behind Denver for 9th)

Don't let the 18th-ranked scoring fool you. The Suns play at the league's 22nd-ranked pace (98.88), so raw points undersell them. Per possession, this is a top-12 offense that shoots it well from deep and takes care of the ball, with a turnover rate (14.6%) that ranks 11th.

Phoenix is 28th in free-throw attempts at 21.2 per game. They're also dead last in fouls drawn at 19.4 per game, so nobody in the league puts less pressure on a defense. Opponents make 21.2 free throws per game against this team. Split it out, and the Suns hit 16.2 per night while opponents hit 21.2. That's a five-point gap at the stripe every game, and the Suns' average scoring margin is -3.4. The free-throw line alone covers more than the entire deficit. Fixing this means attacking the paint more and settling for fewer clean pull-ups.

Now, on to the defense and rebounding numbers.

This is where the season is being lost. The Suns are dead last in rebound percentage and dead last in defensive rebound percentage (62.5%). It isn't close, either: the next-worst team, the Lakers, is at 64.1%. Opponents are grabbing 13.6 offensive boards a night, tied for the 4th-most allowed in the league.

The painful part is that the defense does its job early in possessions. Phoenix forces 17.2 turnovers per game, 5th-most in the NBA. Then they give it all back. They don't finish stops with a rebound. They send opponents to the line 27.4 times a night (22nd), even though they rank 13th in fouls committed, which points to shooting fouls and early trips to the bonus. And they let teams shoot 37.1% from three (26th).

DENVER, COLORADO – NOVEMBER 16: Dillon Brooks #3 of the Phoenix Suns questions a call in the second quarter against the Denver Nuggets at Ball Arena on November 16, 2026 in Denver, Colorado. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and/or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Jamie Schwaberow/Getty Images) | Getty Images

A team that ranks 25th in blocks and 30th on the defensive glass isn't winning the size battle at the rim, and the numbers back up what we see on film. Guards and even wings are leaking out before the board is secured, and nobody is putting a body on the weak-side crasher.

The length shows up in the passing lanes (5th in forced turnovers), so the athleticism is there on the perimeter. It just isn't being turned into box-outs, rim deterrence, or trips to the line. Until someone owns the paint on both ends, every stop costs double the effort, and every bucket comes the hard way.

The net rating is -3.0 (21st), which is a fringe play-in team at best. The good news is the offense is real enough that the ceiling is higher than 11th. Clean up two areas, the free-throw gap and the defensive glass, and this team's overall profile moves toward neutral or better. And more importantly, it wins them games.

The formula for the next game is simple. Five guys hit the glass, the wings stop leaking early, and the offense gets downhill to earn more free-throw attempts. Win the possession battle, and this team is well above .500. Lose it again, and Phoenix stays in play-in territory.

#2790. The Packers have one of the hardest NFL schedules to end 2026

According to ESPN's Football Power Index (FPI), the Green Bay Packers have played an average-ish NFL schedule this season, as they're ranked 16th in strength of schedule league-wide. Coming out of the bye, though, their slate will get a lot tougher leading up to the postseason.

Per FPI, the Packers have the fifth-hardest schedule in the league moving forward, only behind the Los Angeles Rams, Seattle Seahawks, Miami Dolphins and Kansas City Chiefs. For perspective, the other NFC North teams rank sixth (Chicago Bears), 16th (Detroit Lions) and 22nd (Minnesota Vikings).

Moving forward, this is the Packers' schedule:

At the moment, Green Bay sits in the seventh spot in the NFC playoff race with a 6-4 record, a three-way tie in which the Packers hold an advantage over the Seahawks and the Rams. Currently, Green Bay has a 37 percent playoff chance, according to The Athletic's playoff simulator, but a lot will change based on their upcoming game in Los Angeles.

If the Packers win that game, their playoff chances jump up to 59 percent. If they lose, they will drop to 24 percent. That's a huge amount of playoff leverage, because Green Bay is fighting to hold onto that seventh seed against a team with the same record.

For reference, here's the current playoff leverage in each of the Packers' remaining games, in isolation, going into Week 11's action:

Yes, the Rams game means more than the two divisional games left on their schedule, mostly because the simulations only give the Packers a one percent chance to win the division at this point. Being 6-4 in mid-November in one of the toughest divisions in NFL history will do that.

Against non-division opponents, the NFC North is 21-4 this season, an 84 percent win percentage. The best mark for a division in NFL history was the 1984 AFC West, which posted a 78 percent mark. For what it's worth, the best all-time full-season division in NFC history was the 2024 NFC North, when they collectively won 75 percent of their non-division games. Even compared to 2024, the 2026 North is a step ahead.

The Packers still control their own destiny, but this upcoming Rams game is a cornerstone to that path. If they end up losing to Los Angeles, they're going to need some help to get into the postseason — even as a wild-card team. On top of that, they'll need to make up that distance while playing one of the toughest schedules in the league.

Things are shaping up for a very competitive race for a playoff spot between the Rams, Seahawks and Packers, who all have top-five schedules coming out of their Week 11 byes.

#2788. Is Apple the safe haven investors think it is? Here's a way to trade it with less risk

For months, Apple has been the undisputed haven of the technology sector. As the industry grappled with the brutal reality of Nvidia's post-Huang future, investors blindly rotated into Apple, comforting themselves with the narrative that the Cupertino giant was uniquely immune to speculative overspending on artificial intelligence.

While peers bled capital into server farms and data centers. At almost 37x forward earnings estimates, the stock is now priced for perfection, fully valued at precisely the moment the rest of the market is beginning to see multiples contract aggressively. Investors tend to huddle in the shelter of perceived safe havens, but that perceived safety can crack quickly in an adverse news cycle, as Nvidia shareholders recently learned.

One part of the news cycle finally caught up to Apple.

We've recently witnessed a bizarre parade of catastrophic system failures: the massive Microsoft outage, the FAA grounding flights, and cascading disruptions across major banking networks. Through it all, Apple was immune. Until today, when a widespread outage struck, knocking out iCloud, Apple Pay, and core support systems. The official explanation? Yet another internal update gone wrong.

It's undeniably odd how all these major tech infrastructure failures are being chalked up to routine upgrades, all clustering around the exact same narrow timeframe. Without venturing too far into the conspiratorial, the sheer coincidence of these cascading digital blackouts suggests more systemic fragility than we may have realized. That's one thing at less than 10x forward earnings, as Nvidia is, or even at a market multiple. But Apple is twice as expensive as the S&P.

Technically, Apple is balancing on a knife's edge. Shares have been relentlessly hugging the upper end of several channel-based technical indicators, such as the Bollinger Band. Apple has been priced at a steep premium for being immune to what ails other struggling stocks, but is now demonstrating similar vulnerabilities. Investors should ask themselves: what's the upside? That Apple trades at more than 40x forward earnings? 45x? Even at that rich multiple, that would represent only 18-19% upside from here. If the market suddenly decided that Apple's premium to the market would be just 50%, instead of 100%, that would be less than 28x forward earnings, a nearly 27% haircut to the current share price.

Despite the high multiple, options prices are low versus the market. Market implied volatility is well above average (around the 93rd percentile). Apple implied volatility is actually just below the 1-year average. The price of index options is in the 98th percentile relative to Apple options.

Because the stock is nominally expensive (translation? The share price is high in dollar terms), buying outright naked puts is highly capital-intensive. This is exactly where a put spread makes sense. By buying an at-the-money or slightly out-of-the-money put and simultaneously selling a lower-strike put, traders can dramatically reduce the upfront capital cost. More importantly, this structure blunts the negative impact of theta, or time decay, ensuring you don't bleed premium while waiting for the technical breakdown to materialize. Furthermore, taking advantage of the natural put skew—where further out-of-the-money puts trade at relatively higher implied volatility—lets you finance your bearish bet efficiently by selling that inflated premium.

Apple's historic premium is built on a foundation of immunity to the chaos infecting the rest of the market. Today's outage proved the company may be more vulnerable than investors appreciate.

Seth Halvorsen of the Los Angeles Dodgers pitches against the Kansas City Royals during a game at Dodger Stadium on Aug. 11, 2026, in Los Angeles. Getty Images

A former seventh-round pick of the Rockies, Halvorsen was acquired by the Dodgers a few weeks before this year's trade deadline for prospects Nick Frasso and Landyn Vidourek. The hope was that he would be a diamond-in-the-rough addition, similar to Will Klein's emergence the season before.

Instead, Halvorsen struggled in 11 appearances with the team, giving up nine earned runs while striking out six batters. While he pitched better for the team's Triple-A affiliate, going 10 outings without allowing an earned run, he wasn't in the picture for its playoff roster.

Halvorsen might have had a renewed pathway to big-league opportunities in 2027, with several key relievers –– including Evan Phillips, who has since signed with the Braves –– hitting the free agent market this winter.

But now, he will spend all of next year rehabbing instead.

Seth Halvorsen of the Los Angeles Dodgers pitches against the Cincinnati Reds in Cincinnati, Sept. 16, 2026. Getty Images

He remains under team control through 2031.

Halvorsen becomes the third Dodgers player to undergo surgery since the end of the season, joining Klein (who had loose bodies removed from his elbow) and catcher Dalton Rushing (who had an internal brace procedure in his elbow). Shohei Ohtani avoided surgery after his injury-plagued 2026, but his role as a pitcher is still to be determined for next season.

#2784. Fed’s Waller says central bank may need to ‘shock people’ with half-point rate hike

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller on Wednesday called for aggressive action against inflation, saying the central bank needs to "shock people" as prices continue to rise more than he expected.

In the near term, the policymaker told CNBC in a live interview that a half percentage point increase in the Fed's benchmark rate is probably necessary at the Dec. 8-9 meeting of the Federal Open Market Committee.

He said the central bank needs to reverse the three quarter-point reductions from last year and may need to do more.

"I'm very, very concerned about inflation. It's kind of headed in the wrong direction, and it's spreading out," Waller told CNBC's Steve Liesman during a "Squawk Box" interview. "If you really want to get it down, you've got to start taking more aggressive actions."

"You might need to, like, shock people," he said.

Futures prices are fully pricing in a quarter percentage point hike at the FOMC's December meeting. They also are anticipating another move in January, followed by at least one additional increase before the end of 2027, according to the CME Group's FedWatch gauge.

Waller said the Fed wouldn't necessarily have to approve a half-point hike in December if it raised rates by a quarter point and signaled another move coming in January. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh has been against that kind of forward guidance on grounds that it ties policymakers' hands.

"If you're not going to signal this very clearly to markets, you're going in January, my view is then you have to go 50 [basis points] in December to put it in place and get it done," he said.

Recent statements from other central bankers indicate a range of opinions, though most agree that more policy tightening is needed to bring down inflation.

In a speech Tuesday, St. Louis Fed President Alberto Musalem suggested as many as four hikes by the spring of 2027. Some Fed officials, including regional Presidents Beth Hammack of Cleveland and Lorie Logan of Dallas, have endorsed more gradual tightening.

However, continued inflation above the Fed's 2% target might push officials into a more aggressive stance.

The FOMC approved its first interest rate hike in more than three years in September, and then held rates steady at its October meeting. While inflation has continued to run hot since then, other Fed officials seem less eager to drop the policy hammer as heavily as Waller.

"I don't think the situation calls for dramatic action," New York Fed President John Williams said Tuesday in a conversation with Axios. "I think what we've been doing over the last past few months is ... working well. We're collecting the data, analyzing it, having the debate and making a decision."

The Fed in 2025 approved three rate cuts amid worries over the labor market and the belief that inflation would continue to moderate toward the central bank's 2% goal.

However, inflation reignited earlier this year after the strikes the U.S. and Israel launched against Iran in late February. Energy prices spiked while tariff impacts lingered, keeping most inflation readings above 3%.

"First of all, you've got to take back the three insurance cuts we put in last year," Waller said. "If the policy rate last summer in 2025 wasn't bringing inflation down, getting back to that isn't going to bring it down. So we may have to go above ... three hikes plus maybe some more to get it to start actually coming back down."

The FOMC later Wednesday will release minutes from the October meeting.

#2782. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2781. Dodgers suffer unexpected loss as reliever undergoes Tommy John surgery

Last week, Dodgers general manager Brandon Gomes said the team wasn't expecting any other players to have surgery this offseason.

But on Wednesday, some more unexpected injury news emerged.

Right-handed reliever Seth Halvorsen underwent a Tommy John procedure that will sideline him for all of 2027, the club announced. The operation marks the latest setback for Halvorsen, a hard-throwing 26-year-old who has a career ERA of 4.78 in 86 big-league appearances.

Seth Halvorsen of the Los Angeles Dodgers pitches against the Kansas City Royals during a game at Dodger Stadium on Aug. 11, 2026, in Los Angeles. Getty Images

A former seventh-round pick of the Rockies, Halvorsen was acquired by the Dodgers a few weeks before this year's trade deadline for prospects Nick Frasso and Landyn Vidourek. The hope was that he would be a diamond-in-the-rough addition, similar to Will Klein's emergence the season before.

Instead, Halvorsen struggled in 11 appearances with the team, giving up nine earned runs while striking out just six batters. While he pitched better for the team's Triple-A affiliate, going 10 outings without allowing an earned run, he wasn't in the picture for their playoff roster.

Halvorsen might have had a renewed pathway to big-league opportunities in 2027, with several key relievers –– including Evan Phillips, who has since signed with the Braves –– hitting the free agent market this winter.

But now, he will spend all of next year rehabbing instead.

Seth Halvorsen of the Los Angeles Dodgers pitches against the Cincinnati Reds in Cincinnati, Sept. 16, 2026. Getty Images

He remains under team control through 2031.

Halvorsen becomes the third Dodgers player to undergo surgery since the end of the season, joining Klein (who had loose bodies removed from his elbow) and catcher Dalton Rushing (who had an internal brace procedure in his elbow). Shohei Ohtani avoided surgery after his injury-plagued 2026, but his role as a pitcher is still to be determined for next season.

#2780. Broncos activated TE Caleb Lohner from the IR

According to 9NEWS Denver's Mike Klis, the Denver Broncos have activated tight end Caleb Lohner from the injured reserve. Lohner will be at practice today, and this opens his 21-day practice window, during which the Broncos have to decide whether or not to add him to the active roster or keep him on the injured reserve for the remainder of the year.

Lohner, whom the Broncos drafted in the 7th round of the 2025 NFL Draft, earned some early offseason hype due to his performance during the rookie minicamp. Head coach Sean Payton went out of his way to praise Lohner, and had fans anxious to see what he could do throughout the remainder of the offseason program and then training camp/preseason. However, he suffered a lower-body injury during the summer and has been sidelined ever since.

It remains to be seen if Lohner will be added to the 53-man roster and will present the Broncos with a potential wildcard who could contribute to the offense as they make a push for the number one seed in the AFC. The team hasn't had much production from the tight end position, and Lohner is an unknown with elite size and untapped potential as a receiver. Sean Payton has spoken highly of Lohner since he was drafted, so we will have to see what happens with him moving forward.

Right now, the Broncos have veterans Adam Trautman and Evan Engram as the main two tight ends in the offense. Third-string tight end and blocking specialist Nate Adkins leads all tight ends (and the team) with 3 touchdowns but only has 6 receptions in total so far this season. Meanwhile, 7th-round rookie Dallen Bentley made the team, but has been a weekly inactive so far this season. So, there is room for Lohner to make an impact in this positional group if he can show some upside during this 21-day practice window.

Realistically, with his raw background and time missed, we probably will not see any meaningful impact from Lohner this season. However, it would be nice if they gave the Broncos a 6-7 red zone threat for quarterback Bo Nix and the Broncos' offense.

#2779. LeBron Log No. 5: Popcorn in hand, a viral chorus and ‘basketball AI'

LeBron Log No. 5: Popcorn in hand, a viral chorus and 'basketball AI' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James spent Monday night in street clothes.

He still managed to argue a call with popcorn in his hand, practice his golf swing during a timeout and reject a report about himself before the Sixers left Brooklyn with a win.

That followed a Sunday afternoon in which James passed Jason Kidd on the NBA's career assists list and received an unusual description from Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle.

Welcome to the fifth edition of the LeBron Log.

James sat out the Sixers' 135-123 win over the Nets because of low back and sciatica management.

It was his fourth absence through 15 games. James has yet to play both ends of a back-to-back this season after logging nearly 33 minutes Sunday against Indiana.

He watched Monday's game from the end of the Sixers' bench in a yellow hat and an "Ohio Baby" sweatshirt. James snacked from a small bag before switching to popcorn.

When Joel Embiid believed he was fouled on a first-half drive, James stood and pleaded his case to official Jacyn Goble without putting down the box. He also joined conversations between teammates and officials during several timeouts.

James found another way to pass a third-quarter stoppage. As "Hot To Go" played inside Barclays Center, he practiced his golf swing near the bench.

His most pointed contribution came on Instagram.

An ESPN story by Dave McMenamin cited a Sixers source who said James had asked the coaching staff to remain patient while he worked toward finding his rhythm and conditioning by December.

James reposted ESPN's graphic and rejected the account.

"🧢! Those words never came out of my mouth!" he wrote.

The Sixers improved to 4-0 without James. They have beaten the Bucks, Hornets, Raptors and Nets in the games he has missed.

Brooklyn pulled within five points during the final five minutes Monday, putting the Sixers in another clutch game.

Philadelphia has reached 10 clutch wins more quickly than almost any team since the statistic began being tracked in 1996-97. The 2026-27 Sixers are the second team in that span with at least 10 such victories through their first 15 games.

The other was the 1997-98 Atlanta Hawks, who had 12.

James was also on the last team before Philadelphia to open a season with at least 10 consecutive clutch wins. The Lakers started 13-0 in those games last season.

James did not play in the Sixers' latest close finish, but he has now been part of the last two teams to start at least 10-0 under those circumstances.

Before Sunday's game, Carlisle explained why he tries to limit his play calls against James.

"LeBron James is one of the reasons we try not to have play calls, 'cause he knows all of them," Carlisle said. "He's a walking NBA basketball AI machine. That's what he is."

James followed with 15 points, 11 assists and seven rebounds in the Sixers' 131-124 win.

His pass to VJ Edgecombe for a layup with 1:19 remaining gave him 12,092 career assists and moved him past Kidd for third in NBA history. Only Chris Paul and John Stockton remain ahead of him.

Noah Levick wrote more about James' milestone and the anticipation behind his passes.

When told what Carlisle had called him, James appreciated the description.

"So, yeah, Rick is great," James said. "He's one of the greatest coaches of all time, and I've had my battles with him over the years. That's super humbling coming from a guy like that."

Philadelphia has found its soundtrack for James' biggest home moments.

The Sixers have played the viral "LeBron, LeBron, LeBron" song after several of his baskets, including his late putback against the Knicks on Oct. 30.

The refrain comes from a version of Steve Lacy's "Oh Yeah?" that replaces "Come on" with James' name. The Baylor University Men's Choir performed its own rendition, helping the song spread across social media.

Lacy later joined in. During an Oct. 15 concert at Freedom Mortgage Pavilion in Camden, he performed the LeBron version as the crowd sang along.

James has noticed Philadelphia's use of it.

"I've seen it. I've heard it. It's pretty hilarious, to be honest," James said. "I heard it towards the end of a game when we were winning. I made a play, and I heard that song. I was just chuckling to myself on the inside."

#2777. UNC Basketball center Cameron Fens files lawsuit

The 2026-27 college basketball season is two weeks old, and some eligibility questions are still left to be answered from the offseason. That includes North Carolina center Cameron Fens who has yet to play a game this season for the Tar Heels as he's inelgible.

Fens' case was filed in Tennessee, and after uncertainty over the past three months, he's now taking it to another court.

Per Inside Carolina, Fens has filed for a temporary restraining order and a preliminary injunction against the NCAA in the Orange County Superior Court on Wednesday, according to court documents. The site is reporting that the lawsuit contends that amended NCAA Bylaw 12.6, which sets the five-year period of eligibility, makes Fens eligible by its own terms.

He's hoping that he can receive a decision soon and potentially be ready to play Friday against Marshall. The emergency motion for the temporary restraining order will have a hearing that is scheduled for Thursday morning.

North Carolina should know Fens' decision by the afternoon.

For a team that has struggled with rebounding this season, getting Fens eligible could be huge. It should boost the Tar Heels frontcourt which has really struggled this season.

Fens committed to North Carolina this Summer after spending time with South Dakota.

#2776. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2775. Inside India newsletter: Foreign flows helped the market recover. Now it faces the inflation test

Hello, this is Priyanka Salve, writing to you from Singapore.

Welcome to the latest edition of "Inside India" — your one-stop destination for stories and developments from the world's fastest-growing large economy.

As global portfolio managers look to diversify into non-artificial intelligence trades, India, with its strong economic growth and improved corporate earnings, is emerging as a top pick. But the country's inflation, which has been rising for 12 straight months, could impede inflows.

Any thoughts on today's newsletter? Share them with the team.

Foreign investors are returning to Indian equities in record numbers, but the country's accelerating inflation, which last month breached the central bank's comfort level for the first time in two years, could spoil the party.

India's retail inflation rose to 6.09% ​in October, crossing the Reserve Bank of India's 6% upper limit, driven by surging food and transport costs.

Experts told CNBC that businesses have already started passing on increased costs to consumers, with signs of slowing demand visible in the rural economy and expected to hurt urban consumption by early next year.

"Rising inflation will squeeze household real incomes, while higher interest rates will weigh on investment and consumption, potentially putting pressure on corporate earnings," Alexandra Hermann Prasad, lead economist at Oxford Economics, told CNBC.

That could "temper foreign investor enthusiasm in the near term, even if India's structural growth prospects remain attractive," she added.

High-frequency indicators like car sales and credit card transactions softened in September while air passenger traffic declined for the fourth month in a row, Indian brokerage HDFC Securities said in a report on Tuesday.

As per data from the Federation of Automobile Dealers Associations, released on Nov. 6, car sales fell 14.2% year on year in October. The industry body said that its "pressing concern is inventory" with dealers, which was elevated at 45 days in September and October, as against the usual range of 30 days. The inflation print "is not good," Gaura Sengupta, chief economist at India's IDFC First Bank, told CNBC, adding that inflation pressures led by supply-side shocks are spreading, and if that persists, it will affect corporate margins and household consumption.

She expects the RBI to raise benchmark rates by 25 basis points each over the next three monetary policies, taking the terminal interest rate to 6.25% by April next year.

The tightening of monetary policy is also likely to slow the recovery of the private investment cycle, denting India's economic outlook, experts said.

So far in November, foreign investors have pumped in more than $10 billion net in Indian equities. If that sustains, it will be the highest ever foreign capital inflow in a single month.

This would be a sharp contrast to earlier this year in March when foreign investors pulled a record $12 billion from India on worries that the energy shock from the Iran war could squeeze the economy and slow growth.

India's growth story, however, has remained intact so far, with corporate earnings showing a sharp improvement over the last two quarters. That resilience, alongside a rotation from AI names, has drawn investors back to India, as they look to diversify into non-artificial intelligence trades.

The fall in oil prices and the easing of tensions between Iran and the U.S. after the midterm poll results have also worked in India's favor, though experts warn that it is still too early to call November's rebound of foreign capital "a structural reversal."

"November's return of capital inflows primarily reflects lower oil prices, changing global risk sentiment following the US midterm elections, and growing concerns about the sustainability of the AI cycle, rather than a clear structural shift towards Indian assets," Hanna Luchnikava-Schorsch, head of Asia-Pacific Economics at S&P Global Market Intelligence, told CNBC.

That makes rising inflation a big worry for sustained inflows. Higher inflation could further weaken household demand, prompt monetary tightening, and slow earnings growth, making the return on Indian assets "less attractive," Luchnikava-Schorsch said.

India relies on imports to meet 85% of its crude needs and around 50% of its supplies transited through the Strait of Hormuz before the Iran war. Energy supply disruptions due to the Iran war have led to a sharp rise in inflation across several major economies in the world, including India.

"What matters more for attracting stable, long-term capital, and for weathering short-term swings in flows, is whether India can sustain the high growth path it is on," Madhavi Bokil, executive director, credit strategy & standards at Moody's Ratings, told CNBC, adding that higher inflation is a risk to that path.

India's inflation crosses 6% in October after rising for 12 straight monthsIndia's retail inflation rose to 6.09% ​in October, crossing the Reserve Bank of India's 6% limit for the first time under the new series, owing to surging food prices. Inflation has been on the rise for 12 straight months in the South Asian country.

India raises export levies on diesel, aviation turbine fuel amid high inflationIndia reportedly raised export levies on diesel and jet fuel on Monday, while keeping the ‌duty on petrol exports unchanged. The increased levies on exports of key fuel items ​come on the back of rising inflation in India.

Nov.23: HSBC composite flash PMI for November.

#2771. Vance scraps schedule to address tech outages as Trump admin insists incidents aren't linked

Vice President JD Vance on Wednesday abruptly canceled his scheduled appearance at The New York Times DealBook Summit to instead attend meetings on a recent wave of high-profile technology outages.

The cancellation was announced shortly after a senior Trump administration official insisted that the outages, which have affected a wide range of industries and have been felt across the country and around the world, are not the result of a cyberattack or any form of coordinated action.

"We have a great deal of confidence in our assessment that these incidents are the result of upgrades of the systems, systems being patched, hardware being applied" or other "disparate" events, the official said on condition of anonymity to discuss the government's intelligence assessments.

The official briefed news outlets by phone in the wake of numerous major disruptions at large corporations and federal agencies in recent days, including a nationwide aviation ground stop, a protracted Microsoft Outlook outage and a reported blackout of the National Security Agency's classified communications network.

Other incidents include software issues at JPMorgan Chase, an outage at United Airlines that caused some flight delays and product issues at Apple.

On Tuesday, Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton told CNBC that the outages are not a cyberattack and "not a coordinated effort by a nation-state."

He gave a similar assurance on Wednesday to the House Homeland Security Committee.

"We have no indication that these incidents are related, that they're coordinated," Clayton said. "We also have no indication that they're result of a state-sponsored malign actor."

But the sheer volume of incidents in the past week alone has raised alarm bells among consumers and tech experts alike, with some wondering if rapidly advancing artificial intelligence models are to blame.

The senior Trump administration official, asked on Wednesday's call to what degree AI was involved in the outages, said it is "challenging to answer that question across all of these incidents that have been reported."

Sen. Mark Warner, D-Va., vice chairman of the Senate Intelligence Committee, told CNBC on Wednesday morning he wants "better coordination" on AI safety.

"I understand why people are freaked out," Warner said. "The one way we could lose this AI race and [see] all of these companies' valuations plummet dramatically is if we have an AI tool run amok, or an AI-sponsored cyberattack that creates harm."

His comments came hours before before Anthropic is scheduled to price its initial public offering for the first major AI industry IPO.

Warner advocated for a "pre-testing regime" for new AI models that establishes guardrails and helps ensure a "culture of safety built in" to the industry."

He said the U.S. is "lucky" that it has so far avoided a major AI-generated cyberattack or a crisis with a rogue agent that goes beyond incidents like the Hugging Face breach earlier this year.

— CNBC's Samantha Subin contributed to this report.

#2770. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

#2768. Cantor Fitzgerald's Nvidia business interests targeted in Democrats' Lutnick probe

Democrats are scrutinizing the business of Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick's adult sons as they investigate why the top Trump administration official allegedly resisted cracking down on companies suspected of diverting Nvidia's advanced AI chips to China.

The probe unveiled Wednesday ratchets up the pressure Lutnick was already facing from inside and outside the Trump administration. It provides the latest sign that Nvidia, the scandal-scarred AI behemoth, will be embroiled in congressional Democrats' coming anti-corruption crusade, as well as a wider bipartisan effort to clamp down on AI chip sales to China.

Reps. Jamie Raskin, D-Md., and Gregory Meeks, D-N.Y., the respective ranking members of the House Judiciary and Foreign Affairs Committees, are demanding reams of records from Cantor Fitzgerald, the investment firm Brandon and Kyle Lutnick took over from their father after he joined President Donald Trump's Cabinet in February 2025.

"We seek to understand whether any actions or decisions by Secretary Lutnick concerning export controls, enforcement measures, or national security policies may have been improperly influenced by Cantor Fitzgerald's holdings or business interests," the Democrats wrote in a letter sent Tuesday to Brandon Lutnick.

At the time of Howard Lutnick's confirmation, Cantor had amassed a stake in Nvidia reportedly valued around $1.2 billion, one of its largest holdings for its clients. Between September 2025 and March 2026, the firm reported a nearly 50% decline in its Nvidia holdings, a CNBC analysis of securities filings found.

In the same time period, Lutnick had reportedly been pressed to take action against Megaspeed, a Singapore-based tech firm accused of transferring Nvidia AI chips to China. The New York Times reported in October 2025 that the Commerce Department was investigating Megaspeed's ties to Beijing.

Lutnick "repeatedly stood in the way" of implementing controls on Megaspeed and other companies suspected of funneling cutting-edge chips to China, Axios reported Nov. 11.

"Secretary Lutnick's reported obstruction may have resulted in billions of dollars of restricted AI chips being diverted to China," read the Democrats' letter, which was also signed by Rep. Sydney Kamlager-Dove, D-Calif., the ranking member of the Foreign Affairs Subcommittee on South and Central Asia.

Lutnick's reported dithering may also have propped up Nvidia share prices for months, "preventing devastating losses for your company," the Democrats wrote.

"If these media reports are accurate, the American people deserve to know: Did the Secretary of Commerce seriously and perhaps irreversibly compromise U.S. national security interests to make money for his adult children and Cantor Fitzgerald?" they asked.

There is no known evidence that Lutnick's sons had advance or insider knowledge or information about Nvidia or the investigations surrounding it.

Cantor, in a statement to CNBC, called the House Democrats' letter "inaccurate" and said any allegations that its work has influenced government decisions are "baseless."

"Cantor holds no material proprietary position in Nvidia. Cantor's Nvidia holdings arise primarily from equity swaps executed for clients. We buy the underlying shares to hedge those trades. The decline in our holdings since early 2025 reflects the unwinding of those hedges as clients closed or reduced their swap positions, not a Cantor decision to exit a proprietary position," the company said in the statement.

"As a global investment bank, Cantor serves clients across many sectors," read the statement, which called any work with Southeast Asia-based companies "ordinary-course investment banking business."

"We are confident that a full review of the facts will demonstrate that we have operated our business ethically and in full compliance with all applicable rules and regulations," Cantor's statement said.

The new probe is among the first to be announced since the blue-wave midterm election, which will deliver Democrats control of the House and Senate come January.

Nvidia's stock has dropped significantly in recent weeks, straining the tech sector it both leads and helps finance, while raising broader concerns about the stability of an economy that has been fueled by the AI boom.

Jensen Huang admitted to an affair without specifying it was with former Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, whom U.S. officials have reportedly identified as a Chinese spy. Alice Huang and Jensen Huang are not related. Jensen Huang has reportedly engaged in at least two other affairs with Nvidia subordinates. On Tuesday, Jensen Huang wrote on X that he would return as CEO if the company's board asked him back. In its earnings call Tuesday evening, Nvidia Chair Harvey Jones said the board does not intend to reinstate Huang.

In addition to the records, Raskin, Meeks and Kamlager-Dove are demanding Cantor Fitzgerald immediately take "all necessary measures to prevent the destruction, alteration, deletion, or modification of any documents or communications related to this matter." They asked for confirmation by Nov. 30 that appropriate preservation measures have been taken.

Their action illustrates how aggressively Democrats plan to investigate all facets of the Trump administration as they home in on alleged corruption by Trump, his allies and his family.

It's not just Democrats asking questions — Republicans have also demanded answers from Huang, Lutnick and other Nvidia and Commerce officials as the Megaspeed scandal has unfurled. Huang is slated to testify before multiple GOP-led House panels on Dec. 1, while Sen. Tim Scott, R-S.C., has also summoned both Huang and Howard Lutnick to testify.

Earlier Tuesday, House Foreign Affairs Committee Chairman Brian Mast, R-Fla., sent his own letter demanding information from Under Secretary of Commerce Jeffrey Kessler regarding possible chip smuggling by Megaspeed and its affiliate Speedmatrix.

"More and more reports are coming to light of possibly extraordinary levels of chip smuggling conducted by Megaspeed and its broader corporate network, all under Jensen Huang's nose," Mast said in a statement.

#2766. James reaches assists milestone, keeps seeing plays ‘before they actually happen'

James reaches assists milestone, keeps seeing plays 'before they actually happen' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Tyrese Maxey admitted that he wasn't entirely ready for LeBron James' first pass of the afternoon Sunday.

Maxey cut through the paint and James' bounce pass was perfect, but the Sixers' All-NBA guard missed his layup. He's not fully accustomed yet to James' precognitive gifts.

"There's still some times when you're surprised," Maxey said after the Sixers' win over the Pacers. "… I was cutting through just to cut and create space. The ball hit me in the chest and I missed the layup. I was like, 'Ah shoot, my bad.' He was like, 'Yeah, you weren't looking.'"

James has been trying to teach his teammates that he'll spot openings they may not notice.

"I just tell my guys, 'Always pay attention to me when I've got the ball,' because I definitely see plays develop before they happen," James said.

For the most part, James' teammates adhered to his request on the latest historic day in a milestone-filled career. By setting up VJ Edgecombe's layup with 1:19 left in the fourth quarter, James moved into third place on the NBA's all-time assists list, passing Jason Kidd.

The 22-time All-Star recorded 11 assists, his most so far as a Sixer, and now sits at 12,092 in his career. Chris Paul (12,552 assists) and John Stockton (15,806) are the only two players above James.

"It's obviously super humbling," James said. "It's something that I didn't foresee in my career, but I knew assists meant a lot to me. Just the movement of the ball, being able to find my teammates, putting the ball on time and on target.

"And also just being linked with some of the greats that have ever played this game and a dear friend of mine, J-Kidd. It's really special."

James is at or near the top of many all-time leaderboards. He's first in NBA history in points, games and minutes, sixth in steals and fifth in triple-doubles.

Passing has always been a core element of his game.

"First of all, he's got the vision and the awareness," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He's got the size to see. I think he's got some anticipation. Just a half-second before a play happens, he knows how the defense is moving. … And he throws crisp passes. Some of those are cooking out to the corner three guys, so it doesn't let the close-out get there nearly as quickly."

Whether it's a long-range rocket, a drive-and-kick dish or a cross-court feed from the post that suggests he might have eyes in the back of his head, James has always enjoyed finding a wide range of ways to help his teammates.

"For someone that talented, he's very unselfish," Maxey said. "He really prides himself on getting guys open."

As James recalled Sunday, he had exceptional passing tools long before he entered the NBA 24 seasons ago.

"Since I was a kid, I've had the ability to see plays before they actually happen," he said. "Seeing movement, seeing cracks in the defense before they were even open … it's something that's been with me since I was a kid.

"I just had that vision and it just stuck with me throughout my playing days."
