#2740. 4-Star Alabama RB Leaves Program Days After Vanderbilt Game

Alabama RB AK Dear is no longer a member of the Crime Tide football team Getty

Alabama RB AK Dear will likely enter the transfer portal after the season Getty

AK Dear arrived in Tuscaloosa as one of the top running backs in the 2025 recruiting class. Less than two years later, his time with the Alabama Crimson Tide is over.

Head coach Kalen DeBoer revealed during Wednesday's SEC coaches teleconference that the sophomore running back is no longer with the program.

The news comes just four days after the No. 3 Crimson Tide escaped Nashville with a sloppy 21-6 win over the Vanderbilt Commodores.DeBoer didn't offer much of an explanation either.

And for a former blue-chip recruit who was expected to take on a bigger role in 2026, it's a quiet end to a career that never really got started.

GettyAlabama RB AK Dear is no longer a member of the Crime Tide football team

DeBoer kept his answer short when asked about Dear's status.

"AK is a great kid, great player," DeBoer said, via On3. "He's just not with our program anymore."

That was the extent of it. DeBoer didn't provide any other details on why Dear is gone or where he's headed next.

Rumors about Dear's status started circulating on Tuesday, and DeBoer's comments confirmed them less than 24 hours later. Dear was still listed on Alabama's official roster as of Wednesday morning.

He's expected to look for a new program to continue his college career in 2027. If he officially enters the transfer portal, Dear will be the third former blue-chip running back to leave Alabama since DeBoer took over in Tuscaloosa, joining Justice Haynes and Richard Young.

That's not a great trend for a program that has struggled to run the football for two straight seasons.

GettyAlabama RB AK Dear will likely enter the transfer portal after the season

Dear was a consensus four-star prospect out of Quitman, Mississippi, and the No. 2 running back in the country in the 2025 composite rankings.

He backed off his commitment to Ole Miss before choosing Alabama over the Rebels, Mississippi State, and Auburn, among others.

He flashed potential as a true freshman, rushing for 140 yards and three touchdowns on just 19 carries. That includes a 56-yard touchdown run in a 73-0 rout of Louisiana-Monroe.

Dear was in line for a bigger workload this fall. Offensive coordinator Ryan Grubb even praised his growth in August, saying understanding protections "was one of the issues of getting on the field, and he's come a long way."

However, Dear suffered an ankle injury in Alabama's first fall camp scrimmage and missed the first three games of the season. He finally debuted against South Carolina on Sept. 26, then got one more carry against Mississippi State a week later. That was it.

His season stat line: two carries for 6 yards.

Dear stayed on the sideline while Alabama's run game sputtered. The Crimson Tide managed just 54 rushing yards in a 26-10 loss to Tennessee, and Vanderbilt held them to 92 yards on the ground on Saturday.

Alabama will move forward with Daniel Hill, Trae'shawn Brown, EJ Crowell, Kevin Riley, and Khalifa Keith in the backfield when it hosts FCS Chattanooga on Saturday at 1 p.m. CT.

#2739. Anthropic IPO raises over $81 billion in largest offering on record, topping SpaceX

Anthropic priced its IPO at $83 per share on Wednesday, above the expected range, setting the stage for the Claude creator's eagerly awaited Nasdaq listing and a historic moment for the artificial intelligence industry.

The company, along with its seven co-founders, upsized the offering, selling about 980 million shares, according to a press release. That brings the amount raised to $81.3 billion, surpassing SpaceX, which held a record IPO in June. Elon Musk's company raised $75 billion initially and a total of $85.7 billion after underwriters exercised their overallotment.

Anthropic's market cap will depend on total shares outstanding, but the share price would put the valuation above the $1.4 trillion that Anthropic was previously targeting. The stock is expected to begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker symbol "ANT."

The listing is a major test for the AI industry, which has experienced a set of recent shocks, including a dramatic backlash that was felt at the polls in the midterm elections and last week's ouster of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang. The market was already jittery on concerns that rising interest rates will make debt unaffordable for many data center developers and neoclouds.

After publicly unveiling its prospectus in October, Anthropic updated the document last week with an expected price range between $70 and $78 per share.  The company had been planning to sell 825 million shares.

Tech's biggest companies have significant exposure to Anthropic, as an investor, partner or customer. Amazon and Google both own stakes valued at over $100 billion, and a group of tech companies, including Google, Microsoft, Nvidia and Advanced Micro Devices agreed to collectively invest $26 billion at the IPO price. Anthropic, meanwhile, is a buyer of costly systems from Nvidia, Google, Amazon and AMD.

"I think if we do this right, we can create an age of miracles where humanity is able to solve problems that have eluded us for millennia," Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei said in Anthropic's roadshow video. "We have to make sure that all the risks, all the ways that things can go wrong with this technology, we very carefully address those risks as we bring the benefits and as we have a viable business at the same time."

Amodei co-founded Anthropic in 2021 alongside a handful of other researchers less than six years ago, after the group collectively defected from OpenAI. The company is best known for developing a family of AI models called Claude, and it primarily sells its products and services to other businesses.

According to the latest prospectus, each co-founder is selling 3.38 million shares for total proceeds of $1.96 billion.

Anthropic has been racing to maintain an edge over OpenAI and Google in the enterprise market, and it experienced a period of explosive growth in 2026, driven largely by the popularity of its AI coding tool, Claude Code. OpenAI has confidentially filed for an IPO and has said a debut is likely in 2027.

For the nine months ended Sept. 30, Anthropic said, it generated $33.24 billion in revenue, up roughly 1,200% from the same period last year, according to its prospectus. The company reported a $104 billion net loss during the nine-month period, though most of that was due to the change in fair value of convertible notes and derivative liabilities. Anthropic lost $25.1 billion over the same stretch in 2025, the filing said.

Anthropic said in an amended filing that its run-rate revenue was roughly $81 billion at the end of October, up $10 billion from the previous month.

The company has faced plenty of challenges during its meteoric rise over its positions on safety, regulation and national security, leading to two high-profile disputes with the U.S. government this year. Its prospectus included more than 90 pages of risk factors for investors to consider ahead of its IPO.

"Our aim is to help humankind chart a safe course through this technological evolution, open new frontiers of discovery, and bring forth an age of miracles," Anthropic's co-founders wrote in the company's prospectus. "Everyone who owns our stock, whether an institution or an individual, becomes part of that effort."

#2738. Observations after Brown-Maxey duo scores 58 points in Sixers' win without Embiid

Observations after Brown-Maxey duo scores 58 points in Sixers' win without Embiid originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Jaylen Brown-Tyrese Maxey duo starred, LeBron James reached another milestone, and the Sixers earned a win without Joel Embiid on Sunday afternoon.

They improved to 11-3 on the season with a 131-124 victory over the Pacers at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Brown scored 31 points and Maxey posted 27.

LeBron James had 15 points and 11 assists. He passed Jason Kidd on Sunday for third in NBA history with 12,092 assists.

Pascal Siakam scored 31 points for the 5-9 Pacers.

On the first day of a back-to-back, the Sixers were down Embiid (right foot plantar fascia injury management), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pacers' injured players included Tyrese Haliburton and Andrew Nembhard.

The Sixers will face the Nets on Monday night in Brooklyn. Here are observations on their win over the Pacers:

Jabari Walker started for the first time this season, opening the game at center. He's been in the Sixers' rotation the past five games since Bona's injury.

Walker set up the Sixers' first basket, swiping a steal and then tossing the ball ahead to Maxey for a fast-break dunk. Maxey and Brown both began well and combined to score all of the Sixers' first 15 points.

Though he conceded five inches to 7-foot Pacers center Ivica Zubac, Walker was solid in his first stint. He got an early post stop on Zubac and then snatched a strong defensive rebound.

Dean Wade was next up for the Sixers at the five. He sunk a three-pointer on his first shot to give the team a 20-16 lead.

Walker ended up playing 25 minutes and posting 11 points and seven rebounds. Going back to the preseason, his stock has steadily risen.

"Here we are again after another game saying Jabari played very well," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He plays hard, he rebounds, he's got another level of confidence with his shooting this year.

"It's kind of wash, rinse and repeat again with him (today). He was great. … Jabari went in there and battled (Zubac). I thought he certainly matched him, if not outplayed him, which is a heck of a job."

Wade had 12 points and seven boards in 35 minutes. Overall, the Sixers' lack of healthy conventional centers is still a clear deficiency. Of course, everything should be simpler once Embiid is back in action.

The Sixers' defense did not have a sparkling start. The Pacers tallied 35 points in the first quarter and shot 62.5 percent from the field.

Brown helped the Sixers avoid a substantial deficit by draining all sorts of jumpers. He rarely appears bothered by close contests. Brown moved to 7 for 9 from the floor when he knocked down a three early in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope's triple knotted the contest up at 37-all.

Nurse needed to stray from his Plan A rotation when VJ Edgecombe picked up his third foul with 9:56 left in the second quarter. Labaron Philon Jr., who got a game with the G League's Delaware Blue Coats on Friday night, subbed in.

For a rookie guard, Philon has looked comfortable and confident on offense in most of his NBA appearances. He swished a long-range jumper in the second quarter and saw a floater rim out.

Brown reached the 20-point mark and put the Sixers up one with a slam assisted by James late in the second quarter.

He ran into serious foul trouble in the third quarter. Brown was whistled for his fourth foul with 9:53 left in the period for an off-arm shove on Aaron Nesmith. Wade entered and Brown exited.

The Sixers' slim lead didn't slip away. Walker nailed threes on consecutive possessions and Maxey jammed in a dunk.

Soon after, Tacko Fall came in for his first meaningful minutes as a Sixer. The 7-foot-6 two-way contract player grabbed an offensive board off a missed Edgecombe free throw, which drew one of the day's loudest cheers. With Fall on the floor, Maxey and James connected on an alley-oop that extended the Sixers' lead to 82-73.

When the Sixers tried to sit in a zone, Fall was called for a defensive three-second violation. Whatever the scheme, most of Fall's defensive work will come around the paint. He certainly has exceptional physical traits as a rim protector and is not nimble on the perimeter.

Fall logged five scoreless third-quarter minutes and recorded three rebounds. The Sixers outscored Indiana by a point with Fall on the court. Given Fall is occupying a (two-way) roster slot and the Sixers are so depleted at center, it made sense to see a bit of what he can do outside of garbage time. While five minutes isn't enough for any grand conclusions, it's very safe to say Fall is a unique player.

"I'm 7-6, so I can change the game in so many ways," Fall said. "Offensively, setting screens, making sure guys get open, being an easy target for lobs. Defensively, I feel like I've always been an elite rim protector and I've worked on my body a lot to be able to move a lot better."

Nurse praised Fall's outing, saying he "gave us some great minutes and a nice lift."

Fall is appreciative of his first NBA opportunity since the 2021-22 season.

"I've worked very hard," he said. "God has brought me back here and I'm thankful. And I'm ready. Whenever my name is called, I'm going to go out there and do my job."

The Sixers built their advantage to 102-87 with a Brown three early in the fourth quarter. They couldn't cruise to a blowout, though.

Siakam kept hurting the Sixers as a driver and had a highly efficient fourth quarter, scoring 19 points on 7-for-9 shooting. Quenton Jackson's layup trimmed Indiana's deficit to 119-115.

The Sixers ultimately held off the Pacers' push.

Caldwell-Pope canned a three, Wade threw down an and-one dunk, and James checked off his milestone assist with a late dish to Edgecombe for a layup.

#2737. Atlanta Braves Dealt Bad News on Top SP Target in Free Agency

Coming off a Week 10 bye, the 2-7 Eagles will face the Steelers on Sunday.

#2735. Inside Sixers-Nets: How Philly can finish a back-to-back sweep in Brooklyn

Inside Sixers-Nets: How Philly can finish a back-to-back sweep in Brooklyn originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers will arrive in Brooklyn with one victory already secured on their back-to-back and a different combination of stars likely available for the second.

Philadelphia improved to 11-3 with a 131-124 win over the Pacers on Sunday. Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 31 points, Tyrese Maxey added 27 and LeBron James recorded 11 assists to move past Jason Kidd for third in NBA history.

James has not played both ends of a back-to-back this season, making it reasonable to expect him to sit Monday night. Joel Embiid, meanwhile, rested Sunday and is expected to return against Brooklyn. The Sixers had not submitted their official injury report as of Monday morning.

The Nets enter at 3-9, but this is not the same roster that finished 20-62 last season. Before the Sixers attempt to win twice in two nights, here is a closer look at what has changed in Brooklyn and where Monday's matchup could turn.

Nic Claxton is now in Chicago. Julius Randle arrived from Minnesota as the central piece of a four-team trade. Keon Ellis and Moe Wagner joined in free agency, while Michael Porter Jr. remains one of Brooklyn's primary scoring options.

Porter is available after missing Saturday's game for personal reasons. He will give the Nets another shooter after Brooklyn struggled badly without him in a 129-110 loss to Memphis.

The Nets' rotation also includes a large group of young players. Egor Dëmin, Nolan Traore and Mikel Brown Jr. have all received minutes in the backcourt, while Noah Clowney, Danny Wolf and Day'Ron Sharpe have played in the frontcourt.

The combination has produced uneven results. Brooklyn averages 110.2 points and allows 116.4 per game. Its minus-6.3 net rating ranks 23rd in the league.

Brooklyn has shown its offensive ceiling. The Nets scored 145 points in a 34-point win over New Orleans last week. Three nights later, they fell behind Memphis by 26 and lost by 19.

Jordi Fernandez has been direct about the difference between the team he sees in practice and the one that has appeared in games.

"We practice better than we play," the Nets head coach said after Saturday's loss. "It's a frustrating sign because you believe you're better."

The Nets did not merely miss shots against Memphis. Their problems began with their approach.

"We didn't punch first," Randle said. "Just organization. I think maybe just trying too hard, playing a little unorganized, stuff like that. And then just attention to detail. I think we've got a lapse of focus sometimes."

Fernandez was even more direct when discussing his young guards.

"They've got to grow fast," he said. "Being 20 is not an excuse."

That inexperience has appeared in Brooklyn's ball control. The Nets' offensive turnover rate ranks 28th in the league.

They have been much more disruptive on the other end. Brooklyn has forced turnovers on 13.9 percent of its opponents' possessions, tied for seventh.

The Sixers forced 10 Indiana turnovers and converted them into 19 points Sunday. Philadelphia committed 13 total turnovers while playing at the quick tempo it has emphasized since training camp.

Brooklyn has enough length and athleticism to disrupt the Sixers. It has not consistently maintained its own organization after creating that pressure.

Brooklyn has allowed 14.2 three-pointers per game, the eighth-most in the NBA. Opponents have posted a 55.2 effective field-goal percentage against the Nets.

That presents another opportunity for a Sixers team receiving strong outside shooting from several role players.

Anfernee Simons ranks third in the league with 33 three-pointers off the bench and is shooting 41.8 percent from deep. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope has made 46.5 percent of his threes, while Dean Wade is at 41.4 percent.

Maxey made five threes against Indiana and Brown added three. Jabari Walker also continued his strong start by making three of his six attempts from deep.

Those contributions matter on the second night of a back-to-back, especially if James sits. Philadelphia should not need Maxey and Brown to create every possession from a standstill if its shooters continue punishing open space.

Embiid's expected return would change the shape of Philadelphia's offense after James served as its primary organizer Sunday.

Brooklyn no longer has Claxton to use against Embiid. Sharpe has moved into a larger role and is coming off 16 points and 11 rebounds in Memphis. Wagner, Clowney and Randle give Fernandez several other bodies to try, but none presents the same defensive profile as Claxton.

Sharpe's work on the glass deserves attention. The Sixers have survived significant rebounding deficits this season, but allowing Brooklyn extra possessions would give Porter and Randle more chances to settle into the game.

On the other end, Embiid should have opportunities to force Brooklyn's frontcourt into difficult decisions. Sending help would create openings for Philadelphia's shooters. Defending him individually would leave Sharpe, Wagner and Clowney with a demanding assignment.

The Sixers have already won three games without James. Monday could require them to do it again, this time with Embiid returning as the center of the offense.

Brooklyn's record suggests a favorable matchup. Its shooting and young talent make it less comfortable than the standings indicate.

The Sixers handled the first half of their back-to-back by producing a season-high 131 points. Finishing the sweep will require them to adjust to a different available roster while preventing an inconsistent Nets team from finding one of its explosive nights.

#2734. Cleveland Browns: Run defense must right itself in final seven games

The Cleveland Browns have been sound on defense through the first 10 games of the season, with a couple of big exceptions.

The first is the opening drive of the game. Cleveland has allowed a scoring drive in nine games, including six touchdowns, which, coupled with the slow starts from the offense, have left the Browns playing from behind each week.

The second has been the run defense, which has been porous, even by the low standards of the Browns.

Heading into the bye week, the Cleveland Browns' run defense is ranked:

No. 24 with seven runs of more than 20 yards

No. 15 (tied) with eight rushing touchdowns

When they come out of the bye, the Browns will have seven games left on the schedule, starting with a Sunday night tilt at home against the Las Vegas Raiders, and finishing with a road game against the Cincinnati Bengals that could hold major playoff implications.

While Cleveland's defense shouldn't expect an easy game against anyone, it has a chance to stabilize the run defense in the first two games after the bye against the Raiders and Cincinnati Bengals, though it still faces some tricky matchups.

Here is the current ranking and leading rusher for each team remaining on Cleveland's schedule.

Leading rusher: Ashton Jeanty, 123 carries, 380 yards, 3.1 ypc, 1 rushing touchdown

Season high: 102 yards vs. Miami in Week 1

Leading rusher: Chase Brown, 113 carries, 441 yards, 3.9 ypc, 1 rushing touchdown

Season high: 80 yards vs. Houston in Week 2

Leading rusher: Bijan Robinson, 185 carries, 951 yards, 5.1 ypc, 6 rushing touchdowns

Season high: 194 yards in Week 3 vs. Green Bay

Leading rusher: Cam Skattebo, 116 carries, 496 yards, 4.3 ypc, 4 rushing touchdowns

Season high: 124 yards in Week 7 vs. Houston

Leading rusher: Derrick Henry, 185 carries, 699 yards, 3.8 ypc, 10 rushing touchdowns

Season high: 144 yards in Week 1 vs. Indianapolis

Leading rusher: Jonathan Taylor, 177 carries, 739 yards, 4.2 ypc, 8 rushing touchdowns

Season high: 98 yards in Week 1 vs. Baltimore

The Browns are currently holding the final playoff spot in the AFC, with hopes of improving their position over the final seven weeks of the season.

And fixing the run defense will go a long way toward improving their position in the conference.

#2731. The Sixers need to be smart with Jaylen Brown’s hamstring injury

The Sixers staved off a ferocious comeback from the Brooklyn Nets on Monday, but they didn't emerge unscathed. Jaylen Brown suffered a cut on his forehead after running into Julius Randle early in the fourth quarter, although he was eventually able to return to the game. However, he left with what head coach Nick Nurse called left hamstring "tightness" with less than two minutes remaining.

The Sixers have already ruled Brown out from Wednesday's game against the Orlando Magic with what they labeled a "left hamstring strain." He told reporters on Monday that his hamstring "just got a little tight" against Brooklyn, but he didn't seem worried about it.

"I've had a lot of hamstring stuff," Brown said, according to Gina Mizell of the Philadelphia Inquirer. "I don't think it's concerning."

Even if Brown's hamstring injury winds up being much ado about nothing, the Sixers should err on the side of caution this early in the season. They just need to look down the street to DeVonta Smith and the Eagles as a reminder of how quickly a hamstring injury can spiral into a much bigger issue if a player returns too quickly.

ESPN's Kris Rhim reported that Brown is set to be reevaluated ahead of Saturday's game against the Milwaukee Bucks. The Bucks, who are currently 1-13, will be heading into their third game in four nights against the Sixers on Saturday after facing the Miami Heat on Wednesday and Portland Trail Blazers on Thursday. The Sixers, who'll have two days off after facing Orlando on Wednesday, should be able to beat the Bucks with or without Brown.

That game against Milwaukee begins a three-game homestand for the Sixers, who face the Portland Trail Blazers on Monday and the Miami Heat in an NBA Cup game on Wednesday. The Sixers then head to Indiana for their final NBA Cup game on Friday and take on the Houston Rockets on the second night of a back-to-back on Saturday.

After these next two games, five of the Sixers' next six opponents currently boast a winning record. The past week provided them with a much-needed reprieve after a tough start to the season, but their schedule is about to stiffen back up again. It's far more important for them to have Brown available for that stretch than it is these next two games. And if he misses these next two, he'll have nearly a full week to recover.

The Sixers soon may be getting reinforcements in the form of Dominick Barlow, who's set to be reevaluated later this week, according to Tony Jones of The Athletic. "If all goes well, the sense is he is nearing his season debut," Jones added. Barlow is nowhere near the three-level scorer that Brown is, but the Sixers might be able to better survive Brown's potential absence against the Bucks on Saturday if Barlow is able to suit up for that one. (He's already been ruled out of Wednesday's game.)

Brown has missed only one game this season—the Sixers' 98-93 victory over the New York Knicks in both teams' NBA Cup opener—and is fresh off back-to-back 31-point outings. He told reporters Monday that he's "feeling a bit more comfortable… starting to feel a little bit more at home." The last thing the Sixers should do is jeopardize that progress by allowing him to rush back from a hamstring injury.

Unless otherwise noted, all stats via NBA.com, PBPStats, Cleaning the Glass or Basketball Reference. All salary information via Spotrac and salary-cap information via RealGM.

#2730. Bulls Vs Nets Preview: Josh Giddey Benching Fallout

Generally, a matchup between two teams clearly headed for the NBA Draft Lottery isn't that appealing, especially early in the season. However, there is some intrigue heading into the 3-10 Chicago Bulls vs. 3-10 Brooklyn Nets matchup. The storyline primarily centers around one player.

The Bulls made a commitment to Josh Giddey as their lead guard of the future, and things aren't going to plan. Giddey was benched in the Bulls' recent 126-115 loss to the Cleveland Cavaliers after committing six turnovers and having another poor shooting performance.

Giddey has been underwhelming to start the season, averaging 9.9 points on 40.7% shooting from the field and 32 percent from three. He's still a quality playmaker, but his weaknesses-inconsistent shooting, poor defense, and turnovers,-are again at the forefront.

The tough question for the Bulls is what comes next? Backup point guard Tre Jones took advantage of Giddey's benching and finished the Cavs game with 16 points, six assists, and four steals.

The New York Times pondered whether Giddey could move to a bench role with Jones replacing him as the starting point guard. That isn't the ideal scenario for the Bulls because they've made a financial commitment to Giddey, and Jones isn't the long-term answer at point guard either.

Giddey achieved some All-Star buzz at the beginning of last season, though his campaign was derailed by multiple injuries. The Bulls hoped he could make a leap this season, but instead his plus/minus rating is -6.5.

Giddey already has a cloud hanging over his career because the Oklahoma City Thunder replacing him on the roster was the final piece to them winning a championship.

Now, there are serious questions about whether his four-year, $100 million extension could become an anchor for the Bulls moving forward as they try to establish a new culture with Tiago Splitter at the helm and Caleb Wilson and Matas Buzelis as core pieces.

While the Giddey situation plays out, the Nets entered the game against the Bulls hoping for continued development from young players such as Nolan Traore, Egor Dёmin, and Day'ron Sharpe.

Three-time All-Star Julius Randle is auditioning for another opportunity to play for a championship contender. He's averaging 20.9 points per game this season, and the Nets will certainly explore trades for him at the deadline. Randle also has an intriguing player option for next year at just over $35 million.

Then there's forward Michael Porter Jr., who wants to become an All-Star, but that's unlikely to happen in Brooklyn. While Porter Jr. may not have the same urgency to play for a contender because he won a championship with the Denver Nuggets, it's fair to wonder if he gets another chance to play a role on a contender.

Both teams are already looking ahead to next season and evaluating where their veterans fit into those plans.

#2729. Todd Monken: Getting flexed into Sunday night is really cool for our players

If someone had told you at the beginning of the season that the Week 12 game between the Browns and Raiders would get flexed into Sunday Night Football, you probably would have responded with some healthy skepticism.

But with the way the season has unfolded, that’s exactly what’s happened.

With the Browns at 6-4 and the Raiders at 4-6 — and now starting the league’s No. 1 overall pick at quarterback — the two clubs make for one of the most compelling matchups of Thanksgiving weekend that wasn’t already in a standalone window.

Browns head coach Todd Monken told reporters on Wednesday that getting flexed is “really cool for our players.”

“I said it at the beginning of the year. I mean, you want to be on Thursday Night, Sunday Night, Monday Night — you want to be flexed, means you’re having a good year,” Monken said, via transcript from the team. “As nice as it is to have a one o’clock schedule every week, I mean, I said that, early in the [year]. I mean, you’re on a trajectory to be fired at some point because you’re at one o’clock every week. So, you’re fighting like hell to get to where you’re on national TV. So, if people are going to watch football, and more importantly than that, an opportunity to get to seven [wins].”

Monken also agreed that it’s a good test for the Browns as a young team.

“I think you earn where you’re at in the present moment, and that only lends itself to what’s in front of you and how important that’s going to become,” Monken said. “You know, getting the bye at 6-4 and putting yourself in position where the rest of the games matter, and especially the one coming up.”

This will be the Browns’ first Sunday night game at home since 2019, which should make for a rowdy atmosphere on the shores of Lake Erie.

“Oh, it’s a huge advantage. Huge advantage,” Monken said of the anticipated environment. “Making it hard for the opposing offense, cadence-wise, being able to function, and making an advantage for us when we’re on offense. I mean, it’s a huge advantage, and it should be playing at home.”

#2728. How do the Steelers stack up in the AFC playoff picture?

Following an embarrassing performance against the rival Bengals in primetime, it's safe to say that the energy in the Steelers fanbase has once again returned to biblical levels of doom and gloom. And it's hard to blame anyone for feeling that way. After putting together their most well-rounded and physically imposing victory of their own against the Browns before the bye, it's been a bitter dose of reality in the days since the team's 38-18 loss to Cincinnati, in which most well-seasoned Steelers fans could tell the game was all but over after a first quarter in which Pittsburgh possessed the ball for barely a minute.

Before the season, this column predicted that the Steelers would win roughly 10 games, make the playoffs, and potentially win one postseason game if they landed a favorable matchup. And while the Steelers certainly did not feel like a serious team at any point during Sunday Night Football, it's worth pointing out that they are still on track to make the postseason.

Looking at the AFC playoff picture on the NFL's website, the Steelers have a projected 73% chance of making the playoffs. The field for the AFC seems narrowed and set, barring any insane collapses and/or turnarounds. Of the nine teams currently outside of playoff positioning, six of them have three wins or fewer. Two of the remaining three teams are likely without their starting quarterback for the rest of the regular season at minimum (Patriots, Ravens), and the third just turned to a rookie quarterback with a tough schedule remaining (Raiders).

As flawed as this Steelers roster may be, it is still more likely than not that they'll be playing in the Wild Card round. So while the timing might feel off based on the collective bad vibes we're feeling, Sunday's loss makes the following conversation feel that more relevant.

We ranked the top eight non-Steelers team in the AFC playoff picture/bubble based on the criteria: Who could Pittsburgh realistically beat in a playoff game?

Current AFC No. 1 seed; >99% playoff chance per NFL.comRB: Is this finally the Bills' year? Buffalo's struggles to get over the hump to the Super Bowl have been well-documented, but Josh Allen and co.'s ability to win in the Wild Card round (6-1, including a 2024 victory over Pittsburgh) has not been a problem.

The Bills are a juggernaut on offense, ranking first in points per game, fourth in yards, and third in EPA. What always flies under the radar is how Buffalo can win in a number of ways, with the league's second-ranked rushing offense led by James Cook, along with a 13th-ranked passing offense led by perpetual MVP candidate Josh Allen. (The Bills would rank much higher if they threw more, currently third in yards per pass play and fifth in EPA per pass).

However, the Bills still have some proving to do despite a near-perfect 8-1 record, having given up 30-plus points four times already this season en route to a 28th-ranked passing defense. Still the Bills rank middle of the pack in points per game allowed (15) and top 10 against the run (eighth). They have an underrated front and have allowed just 28 points over the last three games, although it has been against the Jets, Vikings and Ravens.

Buffalo fits in that tier of teams where it's hard to imagine Pittsburgh keeping up if a game turned into a shootout. And the Bills' defense's strength being against the run just makes things tougher for a Steelers team that needs to get the ground game going to succeed.

Current AFC No. 3 seed; >99% playoff chance per NFL.com

RP: The Jaguars were my preseason pick to represent the AFC in the Super Bowl, and that feeling has only increased for me since then. With respect to Josh Allen and the Bills, the team from Duval gets my vote for most difficult matchup, largely because I believe them to be the most well-balanced team in the conference this year.

Whether you're a fan of modern analytics or more traditional stats, this Jaguars club favors well. They are ranked fourth in both offensive EPA per play (+0.10) and opponent EPA allowed per play (-0.18). They rank first in point differential (+125). They score the fourth-most points per game (29.9) while allowing the fourth-fewest (16.0). And they are both ninth in total yards per game gained (358.9) and allowed (319.2).

On offense, this team motions receivers at the NFL's fourth-highest rate (75%) to create favorable matchups, and then they're bombs away with the highest frequency of passes traveling over 10 air yards (43%). On defense, the Jaguars live in base defense (4-3) at a higher rate (52%) than all but three teams. They don't blitz a ton, but still generate a high rate of pressure. Long story short, the Jaguars have the type of offense that can give the Pittsburgh secondary fits, while still winning enough on the ground. On defense, the Jaguars rely on a base defensive look that can counterpunch the Steelers' running game while still smothering the passing game. We'll dive into this team in further detail when these teams play in Week 14, but suffice it to say, this is a scary matchup for Pittsburgh.

Current AFC No. 2 seed; 97% playoff chance per NFL.com.

RB: The Chiefs are largely back after a disastrous 2025, currently ranking as the AFC's No. 2 seed with a 7-2 record. That's scary news for the rest of the NFL, as the head coach/quarterback combination of Andy Reid and Patrick Mahomes has far more postseason experience and success than the rest of the field combined.

The Chiefs' offense, top 10 in both points and yards this season, has Mahomes averaging eight yards per attempt for the first time since 2022, even if he still isn't pushing the ball down the field much. And the addition of Kenneth Walker III, now an Offensive Player of the Year frontrunner, has given the Chiefs an elite running back for the first time in the Mahomes era. Don't discount the recent trade for Josh Downs, either, which provided a much-needed reinforcement to KC's group of pass catchers.

The Chiefs' defense is once again competent, in the upper half of the league against the run and pass and top six in points and EPA per play allowed. Kansas City is blitzing less than in past seasons (a 17th-ranked 30.6% this year) but coordinator Steve Spagnuolo remains one of the league's most innovative minds when it comes to pressure, and he's not afraid to use man coverage, either — not a great matchup for Pittsburgh.

The one saving grace of facing the Chiefs for the Steelers is that a wide receiver room of Rashee Rice, Downs, and Xavier Worthy isn't as terrifying for a reeling Pittsburgh secondary as most of the other teams on this list. But this is not a team or coaching staff you want to be facing in January.

Current AFC No. 4 seed; 98% playoff chance per NFL.com

RP: We don't need to drag this one on too long; the wounds are still fresh. As long as Joe Burrow, Ja'Marr Chase, and Tee Higgins are healthy, this matchup is a coin flip for the Steelers on the best of days, and a complete mismatch on their worst. Joe Burrow is turning in an MVP-level performance this season, despite every team knowing exactly how the Bengals plan to attack them. He can pick apart zone coverages, and the All-Worldly duo of Chase and Higgins can win matchups against man coverage more often than they lose. If secondary weapons like Chase Brown and Mike Gesicki bring their A-games to a contest, it's all the worse for the opposing defense.

The Bengals defense is still streaky. The defensive line is currently its strength, but the linebackers are gettable in both phases, and the secondary lives and dies by the pass rush's performance. Bryan Cook and Jordan Battle are an above-average safety tandem this year, but the cornerbacks have been up and down. The Bengals' defense is also pretty terrible against the run, with the sixth-worst EPA allowed per rush in the league. As such, they've had some bad performances, even recently. It was only a couple of weeks ago that a putrid Tennessee offense hung 36 on this unit, and an abysmal Dolphins squad managed 24 points against the Bengals. Heck, even the Steelers managed 30 points in their first meeting.

If an opposing offense comes to play, especially with a strong running game, beating the Bengals in a shootout is more realistic than you might feel following Sunday's loss. But do the Steelers have that level of discipline, let alone consistency? That's the part that leaves me unsure, especially after the Mason McCormick injury.

Current AFC No. 5 seed; 97% playoff chance per NFL.com

RB: The Broncos are a tough team to gauge. On one hand, they're 7-2 with a top-10 scoring defense in one of the league's tougher first-half schedules. On the other hand, they have a bottom-six offense and defense in terms of yards, and a quarterback in Bo Nix who most analysts agree is a few tiers below Mahomes, Allen, Lawrence, Burrow and CJ Stroud. Saying a team "just wins" isn't always a great argument for postseason success.

Creating the fourth-highest pressure rate in the league (39.7%), the Broncos defense comes with a dangerous front (Zach Allen, Nik Bonitto, Jonathon Cooper) and a secondary with a lot of talent when healthy (Patrick Surtain II, Talanoa Hufanga, Ja'Quan McMillian). Good news for the Steelers is you can run on Denver, with the Broncos giving up the sixth-highest yards per rush in the NFL this year (4.5).

On offense, the Broncos have a good foundation with one of the top offensive lines in the league and an experienced play caller in Sean Payton. However, the Broncos' 26th-ranked rushing offense has struggled to find consistency (although the emergence of Jonah Coleman is promising), while the trade for Jaylen Waddle has failed to provide a spark in a 25th-ranked passing offense. Nix can run and has the arm to uncork deep shots, but continues to heavily rely on short, outside the numbers throws. He has the second-lowest air yards per attempt of qualified passers in 2026.

To me, this feels like the first team on the list where Steelers fans might have some confidence in a playoff matchup. Facing this team and defense in Denver isn't an enviable task, but the Broncos offense is the least intimidating on this ranking so far. Still, Denver is tied for allowing the lowest sack rate in the league this season, with the quick passing game nullifying some of the dangers of opposing pass rushes. We'll get to see how these teams match up on Black Friday.

Current AFC No. 11 seed (currently out of playoffs); 23% playoff chance per NFL.com

RP: It's a bit funny that the Texans rank this high. With just three wins on the season, and coming off an embarrassing loss to the Browns in which they blew a 17-0 lead, it's mostly a nod to how bad of a matchup this feels like it would be if Houston were miraculously able to turn the ship around and sneak into the playoffs. As things stand, for the Texans to get in, it would likely require a brutal collapse in the second half of the season from either the Steelers or Browns, or an even more devastating collapse from the Jaguars, Chiefs, Bengals or Broncos.

Still, this is a team on paper that should give the Steelers problems, and they did so just a season ago in the playoffs. While the Texans defense has not lived up to expectations this season, they still have a fearsome pass rush led by Will Anderson Jr. and Danielle Hunter, a feisty secondary, and a stingy run defense. On the other side of the ball, C.J. Stroud has been one of the hottest quarterbacks this season despite the team's record. He's top-seven in both passing yards and touchdowns, and his three interceptions are the fewest among quarterbacks with at least 300 dropbacks this season. Receiver Nico Collins has the exact size and skill set that has bullied this secondary this season, and the Texans have an interesting stable of secondary receivers to prevent defenses from focusing solely on him.

They may be highly unlikely to make the playoffs at this point, but they aren't a team I'd be uber confident the Steelers could beat in January. Like Jacksonville, we'll dive into Houston in further detail in a few weeks when these teams play.

Current AFC No. 7 seed; 56% playoff chance per NFL.com

RB: Like with the Bengals, the Steelers have split their series with Cleveland this season, with the most recent matchup being a blowout. However, in this case, that game went in favor of the Steelers, who defeated the Browns 27-6 in Week 8 in what has been Pittsburgh's strongest game on both sides of the ball so far this season.

The Browns rank in the bottom half of the league in both yards and points per game on offense, but still have a bevy of exciting young playmakers and a quarterback in Deshaun Watson who has risen to the moment in some big games this season for Cleveland, which is shockingly 6-4 entering a Week 11 bye.

On defense, the Browns rank in the middle of the NFL in terms of yards and points, but with a bottom-10 rushing defense that shouldn't surprise Steelers fans. Still, Cleveland has a sixth-ranked passing defense that lives heavily in man coverage, something that bit the Steelers the first time the two teams met this season.

The Browns' potential on offense and ability to shut down the Steelers' passing game — not to mention that they're a division rival — still makes this a somewhat worrisome potential matchup for Pittsburgh in the playoffs. But it's still not every day you get to play a team in the postseason that you've already beaten by 21 points earlier in the year.

Current AFC No. 10 seed (currently out of playoffs); 15% playoff chance per NFL.com

RP: This almost feels disrespectful. Truthfully, this might just be a case of out of sight, out of mind for now. But the Ravens have also been struggling lately, including a loss on Monday Night Football to the previously winless Chargers. The case here is simple enough to make. This team isn't the same without starting quarterback Lamar Jackson, and it's looking unlikely that he'll return from injury quickly enough to save Baltimore's season.

If they can gut out some tough wins with the recently acquired Jacoby Brissett at quarterback, and then beat this Steelers team twice, Baltimore might sneak in. But their remaining schedule isn't an easy one: Carolina, Houston, Tampa Bay, Pittsburgh, Cleveland, Cincinnati, Pittsburgh. At 4-6, they have little margin for error, and they haven't scored more than 20 points in a game since Oct. 11.

I'm not one to count the Ravens out until they're officially out of things, but also looking at the rest of the AFC playoff hopefuls, it's hard to say Baltimore without Lamar isn't the best possible matchup. Still, it'd be an unlikely one, as they're most likely to steal a spot from a fellow AFC North team. I don't see Cincinnati falling out of the race if they remain healthy. And even if the Steelers rebound and somehow win the AFC North, they'd most likely be the fourth seed and would play the fifth. The current fifth seed, Denver, is 7-2. Hard to see this Ravens squad getting that high, so this is likely a moot point.

Also on playoff bubble: Patriots (5-5; No. 8 seed; 36% playoff chance); Raiders (4-6; No. 9 seed; 17% playoff chance).

#2727. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

#2726. LeBron James denies telling Philadelphia to be patient as he finds rhythm

It all started with this paragraph, written by ESPN's Dave McMenamin, in a story about what is working and not working for the Philadelphia 76ers as they start the season.

A Sixers source told ESPN that James has asked coaches to be patient with him as he expects to find his rhythm and get his conditioning in line by December as he ramps up.

ESPN's social media team clipped that quote into an Instagram post — then LeBron James saw it and denied ever saying that.

It's an interesting reaction to a report that wasn't so much critical of James as it was explaining his slower statistical start. LeBron is averaging a career-low 15.1 points per game (his previous low was 20.9). He's doing that while shooting 44.3% overall and 22.5% from 3, with a 51.8 true shooting percentage, all numbers that are career lows or close to it. That said, he has been the glue for this star-studded roster in many ways, and he leads the team in assists at 6.9 per game — he has been especially critical for them at the end of clutch games, where the 76ers are 9-0.

It's also worth noting that McMenamin wasn't directly quoting his source, it read more like his source was relaying the essence of a conversation. LeBron didn't like the interpretation used.

With the 76ers at 12-3 and on top of the East through 15 games, LeBron and Philadelphia can afford to be patient, whether he asked for that or not. If the 76ers are going to be playing in late May and June as they hope, it's going to take a healthy and efficient LeBron — as well as Joel Embiid, Jaylen Brown and Tyrese Maxey — to make that a reality.

#2724. Boston Celtics star Jayson Tatum hears criticism, but isn’t moved

Boston Celtics forward Jayson Tatum didn't become a superstar over night, but it was a quicker development than usual. The former Duke Blue Devil dazzled in his rookie season and nearly led the C's to the NBA Finals in 2018 — all before turning 21 years old. From that point on, especially as Boston established itself as a perennial contender, Tatum was put under a microscope.

The media hyper-analyzed his relationship with former Celtics star Jaylen Brown (a practice that continues to this day), attacked him for not being flamboyant enough, and questioned his clutch factor. The criticism persisted even when Tatum helped Boston end a 16-year title drought in 2024, as many online commenters mocked his post-championship celebration, labelling it disingenuous and saying he was copying other NBA greats, including Celtics legend Kevin Garnett and five-time Los Angeles Lakers champion Kobe Bryant. Although the 28-year-old rarely addresses those who ridicule him, he laughed when asked about them during an interview with Boardroom. Tatum then collected himself before providing a thoughtful answer:

"Listen, I'm all for the trolling and the funny memes and videos, especially on social media," he began. "Does it invoke anything in me? No, not at all. I understand the place that I hold in the NBA and oftentimes (that) comes with a lot of praise, and you got to take your fair share of moments where people want to talk (expletive) about you."

For Tatum, achieving his basketball dreams in exchange for online backlash seems like a worthwhile tradeoff. He emphasized that all of the best players in the world are critiqued, regardless of whether the flak is warranted.

"You can't have it just one way," he said. "And I think...that's part of being a superstar. People on the Internet got a lot of time on their hands."

While some stars take it upon themselves to confront trolls on social media, Tatum has always preferred to settle things on the court. Perhaps this spurning of often faceless detractors is why he draws so much ire online, but also why he commands the respect of basketball's biggest names, including the likes of LeBron James, Kevin Durant, Chris Paul, and others.

"(Expletive) it, we won," Tatum said in response to those claiming his joy after winning it all was performative. "I don't care what y'all say."

To put it simply, Tatum is more than an NBA champion and a six-time All-Star. He's a father who prioritizes his children over engaging with manufactured drama.

His reaction to Brown calling him a liar on Instagram — which has been one of the biggest media controversies of the early season — confirms that.

"I understand (it) being the entertainment business," Tatum told reporters in late October. "Personally, I'm not much of an off-the-court entertainer type of guy...I got two kids."

#2722. The early reviews for the Madden movie aren't good

The Madden movie, starring Nicolas Cage as the legendary coach, broadcaster, and video-game mogul, has landed on Prime Video. In the eyes of critics, it has landed with a thud.

The current consensus at Rotten Tomatoes, based on 16 reviews, is 13 percent.

The folks at Sports Business Journal have collected and summarized some of the feedback. Owen Gleiberman of Variety calls it a “stunt biopic on cinematic amphetamines,” adding that “it’s like an SNL sketch shot and edited by Scorsese.”

Then there’s this incredible lede from David Rooney of the Hollywood Reporter: “Imagine being cornered at a party while sober by a wired cokehead determined to tell you a long, painstakingly detailed story without pausing for breath and you might have some idea of the exhausting experience of watching Madden.”

Bryan Curtis of The Ringer says "[i]t’s not quite as awful as the trailers made it look,” but that it still “isn’t good.” Curtis also says Christian Bale is “mesmerizing” as Al Davis, which raises the question of why they didn’t just make an Al Davis movie?

Of course, I’ll still watch it. You should, too. But I have a feeling that, throughout the experience, I’ll be wondering how much John Madden himself would have hated it.

#2721. Luka Doncic leadership on display as Lakers unexpected starter went bonkers

Luka Dončić recognized what was happening.

He has been at the center of so many scoring flurries. He can tell when someone is about to have a hot hand. When an explosion is about to happen.

So when he saw the telltale signs coming from one of his teammates, he jumped into action, waving off Laker coach JJ Redick's play calls in the fourth quarter.

"No, I'm trying to get Q the ball," Doncic told Redick. "I'm trying to get Q."

Luka Doncic of the Los Angeles Lakers reacts after scoring against the Charlotte Hornets on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026, at Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles. Getty Images

Quentin Grimes went on to have the most spectacular performance of his career, scoring a career-high 47 points in the Lakers' 118-114 overtime win over the Hornets.

"I got going and caught fire a little bit," Grimes said.

The 26-year-old guard had 21 points in the fourth quarter, singlehandedly outscoring the Hornets (17 points) in that period to help the Lakers claw their way back from an 18-point deficit.

On a night when the Lakers were without Austin Reaves and Walker Kessler, Grimes took over. On a night when Doncic was in foul trouble, Grimes played the hero.

It was obvious that Grimes was having a different kind of night at the top of the fourth quarter.

In a 23-second stretch, Grimes made a 3-pointer, stole the ball from Ryan Kalkbrenner and then drained another shot from beyond the arc to cut the Lakers' 12-point deficit to 6 points with 9:04 left.

Los Angeles Lakers guard Quentin Grimes reacts after making a 3-point shot during a game against the Charlotte Hornets on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026, in Los Angeles. Getty Images

From that moment forward, it was clear the ball was flying at a higher arc than usual when it left Grimes' fingertips. The rim had seemingly opened a little wider. He was having one of those nights where the ball seemed magnetized to the bottom of the net once it left his hands.

He went on to score 15 points in a row for the Lakers.

So, when the Lakers found themselves down 111-108 with 12.1 seconds left, there was no doubt who was going to take the final shot.

It wasn't the Slovenian superstar who's leading the league in scoring with 39 points per game.

It was the journeyman who had been traded three times since the LA Clippers selected him with the 25th pick in the 2021 draft, playing for the New York, Detroit, Dallas, Philadelphia and the Lakers over six seasons.

Quentin Grimes (5) of the Los Angeles Lakers dribbles during a game against the Charlotte Hornets in Los Angeles on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

After Matisse Thybulle chased down a missed free throw by Coby White, he passed the ball to Doncic, who dribbled down the court and stopped at the top of the 3-point line. Doncic pump-faked, then passed it to Grimes.

Grimes swished a 26-foot 3-pointer with 1.7 seconds left, sending the game into overtime with the score knotted at 111-111.

The shorthanded Lakers went on to pull off their ninth straight win, improving to 13-2, the best record in the league.

"What an amazing game from him, so it was unbelievable to watch," said Doncic, who had 36 points, six rebounds, seven assists and five fouls.

Quentin Grimes #5 of the Los Angeles Lakers shoots a three-point basket during a game against the Charlotte Hornets on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026, in Los Angeles. NBAE via Getty Images

Grimes shot a stunning 10-for-14 from beyond the arc, becoming the only Laker to score 10 or more 3-pointers in a game other than Kobe Bryant.

"Man, I'm going to sleep good knowing that one tonight for sure," Grimes said. "But that's just crazy. Kobe's one of my idols growing up watching him. You try to model your game after him and his intensity, his passion for the game, that's just something that you don't see often.

"He's one of the reasons that I wanted to play this game. My dad was a Lakers fan from here and everything. So that's just crazy to have my name in a conversation with a legend like that."

After missing two games because of a sprained right pinky, Grimes returned Sunday against the Suns and went on to bank a game-sealing 3-pointer with 32.2 seconds left to lift the Lakers to a 129-121 win.

When asked if he called bank, he joked that he called game.

Then against the Hornets, Grimes had his magnum opus, surpassing his 46-point performance with the 76ers in March 2025.

"The hot-hand theory, I believe was prior debunked," Redick said. "Now it's been confirmed that it's a real thing. It definitely was a real thing tonight."

For Grimes, it was the type of game that can imbue him with confidence for an entire season.

It was the type of game that can tilt his trajectory.

"I'm calling this a statement game," he said.

#2720. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2718. When the season expands to 18 games, reseeding will likely follow

With the NFC South destined to send a none-of-the-above team to the playoffs and to have that team host a playoff game, there inevitably will be criticism of the current structure of the postseason. And it inevitably will dust off the proposal the Lions made two years ago, with a nudge from the league office, to reseed the seven teams based on record and not on division championship.

When will the talk become action? When the NFL expands to regular season to 18 games.

That’s the current thinking, as we’ve consistently heard it. Expansion of the regular season will result in reseeding of the playoffs,

Winning the division will still get a team in. But whether that team hosts a playoff game will depend on whether its record is one of the four best in the conference.

The best approach would be to realign the league to four eight-team conferences. Four division champions, 10 wild-cards. No arbitrary separation of AFC and NFC.

We won’t hold our breath for that one to happen. The league takes baby steps, not giant leaps. And when it takes its next step to 18 games, wild-card teams likely will be able to leap lesser division champions.

#2716. Devin Carter speaks for first time since preseason illness

DENVER — Devin Carter was just beginning to hit a groove. The Celtics guard, traded from the Kings to the Hawks over the summer and waived days before training camp, had a stellar preseason debut in Cleveland.

On a training camp deal, Carter finished with 17 points, 5 rebounds, 3 assists, and 2 steals. But just a few days later, he got sick and was diagnosed with mononucleosis, an illness he deems was the worst he's ever had.

"I definitely was upset at the timing that it happened because, like you said, I had a good game in Cleveland, and I was looking forward to playing the rest of preseason and showing what I worked on all summer," Carter said at Celtics shootaround on Wednesday morning. "And then everything gets stripped quick."

After nearly a month sidelined, the former lottery pick is back on the court. He lost 10 pounds while sick, and he still finds himself a bit more fatigued than usual. The whole experience was completely foreign to him.

"I never had COVID. I never had nothing," he said. "So this is the first thing I've really ever been sick with."

In the last few weeks, though, Carter has turned a corner. He made his G League debut earlier this month and logged 12 minutes on Sunday in his Celtics regular-season debut against the Dallas Mavericks.

"The G League has actually been very helpful so far — getting my wind back and getting reps in that I missed out on for four weeks," he said. "So it's been very helpful."

Still, while he's cleared and back out there, he's still not 100% himself.

"I still feel fatigued, more so, energy's low," Carter said. "You play two minutes, everything just shuts down, and then just building that back up. Obviously, the way I play, it's kind of tough to not be tired. So on top of the way I play, dealing with the sickness is just tough. But I'm not in a rush. I definitely feel myself back in shape and playing a lot better."

Carter said he's kept a positive perspective throughout the illness.

"It's been a blessing, regardless," he said. "Everybody gotta go through something to get where they want to go… definitely coming back into my body."

He's grateful the organization has supported him through the unexpected setback that came just weeks after he joined the squad. Celtics head of player development Craig Luschenat, for example, has been sending him clips from his G League film, and he's received game edits throughout his time sidelined.

"They've been very supportive," he said. "Front office, coaches, players, everybody been supportive of me. Everybody's helped me out. Anything I need — Advil, water, everybody's been super helpful."

As he works to get back into his usual shape, he's focused on carving out a role in the NBA after he was cut just two years into his career.

"Just picking up full court, being more of a tight defender, taking care of the ball, trying to force turnovers, playmaking, whatever the team needs in the moment, guarding the best player," he said. "I think I do a good job of just doing the little things that help a team win, and I mean that's ultimately what every team in the NBA needs — somebody who can do the little things… You have the superstars and those people who put the ball in the basket. You have to have the surrounding cast."

For now, he's grateful that he found Boston as a landing spot, a place that's deeply impressed him since he first arrived.

"Everybody wants to show up," Carter said. "Everybody wants to be around people. Everybody wants to get better. And chemistry on the team is at an all-time high. I've never been a part of a team with chemistry like this, so we do stuff off the court, and it's just great to be around."

Tight end Will Mallory (hamstring), offensive tackle Bernhard Raimann (concussion), defensive tackle Jerry Tillery (calf), and offensive tackle Jalen Travis (knee) have also been ruled out for Week 11.

Receiver Anthony Gould (toe), offensive tackle Charlie Heck (personal), and long snapper Luke Rhodes (hamstring) have been listed as questionable.

Guard Dalton Tucker (shoulder) has no game status and is expected to play.

#2713. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

#2712. NFL blasts Mike Florio’s ‘inappropriate’ Mike Vrabel-Hitler joke

The NFL isn't happy with Mike Florio's apparent Adolf Hitler joke.

During Tuesday's episode of "ProFootballTalk Live" after the Patriots' ugly loss to the Lions in Germany, Florio and his co-host Chris Simms laughed back and forth about the New England head coach's facial hair, with the latter saying he had a "German mustache."

Florio, an NFL insider for NBC Sports, went along with the gag.

New England Patriots head coach Mike Vrabel gestures during a press conference after an NFL football game in Munich, Germany, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/Matthias Schrader

"You gotta be careful trimming that thing," Florio said. "…If you're in Germany, you gotta be careful trimming the sides of that thing."

Florio, who never mentioned Hitler by name, doubled down after apparently getting a reaction out of the show's producer.

"Gary's groaning," Florio said. "It's funny, Gary. It was 80 years ago. Let us have some fun, for crying out loud."

The exchange didn't go over nearly as well at the league's headquarters.

"We found the remarks inappropriate and offensive," the NFL's executive vice president of communications and public affairs Jeff Miller said in a statement to The Post.

A league source told The Post that the NFL took its concerns about Florio's remarks directly to NBC Sports, one of its longtime broadcast partners.

Asked about potential disciplinary actions, the source said the league had "expressed displeasure with his remarks."

Mike Florio stands on the field before an NFL game between the Baltimore Ravens and Kansas City Chiefs in Kansas City, Mo., Thursday, Sept. 5, 2024. Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

Florio and Simms responded to the backlash on Wednesday.

"On yesterday's 'PFT Live' show, we made comments that some found offensive and we apologize," they said in a statement posted by NBC Sports.

Florio also serves as an insider on the network's "Sunday Night Football" pregame show, "Football Night in America," a role he has held since 2010.

Sunday's game at Allianz Arena in Munich, a 35-10 Lions victory, marked the sixth NFL regular-season contest played in Germany.

The NFL has played at least one regular-season game in Germany every year since 2022.

#2709. Vikings-49ers First Injury Report

The Minnesota Vikings and San Francisco 49ers released their first injury reports in advance of their matchup on Sunday night at the Azteca in Mexico City. The 49ers held a walk-through in their temporary location in Colorado, while the Vikings held a regular practice.

PUP = Physically unable to participate list.

IR = Injured reserve. Note: only starters on IR are listed.

Probable = the league defines a Probable injury designation as a 75% or greater chance of playing. Historically, 95% of players designated probable are active and played on game day.

Questionable = the league defines a Questionable injury designation as being 'uncertain' of whether the player will play or not. This replaces the former definition of having a 50% chance of playing. Historically, 70-75% of players with a Questionable injury designation were active and played on game day.

Doubtful = The league defines a Doubtful injury designation to mean the player is unlikely to participate in the game. In practical terms, teams use the designation when a player has less than a 50% chance to suit up, and active game appearances from this designation remain rare. Historically only around 6% of players with a Doubtful designation are active and played on game day.

Out = will not play. Players with this designation are not active on game day and league rules prohibit them from playing.

For the Vikings, it looks like just Jordan Addison and Kyler Murray will be out for Sunday's game. Addison just had thumb surgery and won't play on Sunday while Murray won't be active either with his hamstring injury.

Cashman, Ingram-Dawkins, Hockenson, and Jauan Jennings are all on track to practice and play on Sunday.

Van Ginkel and the rest look like they have some minor injuries they are playing through.

For the 49ers, beyond the IR players both Mike Evans and Dre Greenlaw didn't practice today (although Evans was seen doing a few very limited things on a side field). 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan said he hoped Evans would practice later this week and hoped he could go on Sunday. For now he looks more doubtful but we'll see how he progresses during the week. Getting him back would be big for the 49ers offense. Brock Purdy's EPA/dropback has been roughly twice as high with Evans in the lineup than without. Evans has missed the last three Niners' games with Achilles tendonitis.

Renardo Green is also one to keep an eye on as a starting cornerback. He has been dealing with a back injury for a couple weeks now and had to leave the Cowboys game on Sunday because of it. We'll see how it goes with him and Stout- another cornerback limited to start the week. My guess is that Green will be questionable on Sunday and if he plays he won't be 100%. Stout has been dealing with an AC joint issue for several weeks now. The neck issue is new.

The others look to be dealing with minor injuries they can play through or aren't starters so less impact one way or another.

We'll have updates tomorrow and Friday with injury designations.

#2708. Todd Bowles: Baker Mayfield's made progress, decision on playing to come on Friday

Buccaneers quarterback Baker Mayfield missed practice on Wednesday with a foot injury, but the team hasn’t made a decision on his availability for Sunday’s game against the Lions.

Mayfield suffered the injury during Tampa’s Week 9 loss to the Bears and he recuperated during the bye week. Head coach Todd Bowles said that has allowed him to improve enough for the team to leave the door open for him to play this weekend.

“He’s made a lot of progress,” Bowles said, via the team’s website. “We want to see him do some things later on today and tomorrow. We’ll kind of make that determination on Friday. But he’s a lot better than he was a week ago. I don’t know if that’s ready or not. I want to see it for myself and we’ll see what he can do tomorrow.”

Rookie Jalon Daniels worked with the first team on Wednesday. Easton Stick was signed to the active roster and the Bucs also added Cooper Rush to the practice squad this week.
